# HMTc Category 21 (Children's Toys, Arts, and Crafts) — standard derivation report
Method: HMTc Method v2.0 — government-limit default. Every cell is the strictest applicable sovereign
maximum for this exact product form in its native basis, a disclosed read-across where the exact cell
is unregulated, a substantiated not-material determination, or a control. No cell is occurrence-derived.
READING THE STRICTER-UNSELECTED FLAGS. The audit scan compares every credible-regulator register row whose product scope matches this category against each cell's binding value and flags anything stricter that was not named. Three families of flag recur and each has a single standing answer. (1) SIBLING MATERIAL CATEGORIES. Directive 2009/48/EC Annex II Part III point 13 sets three limit sets for the same instrument, keyed to material form, and all three share a product scope, so on a Category III row the Category I and Category II values are always flagged as stricter. They are inapplicable: a limit for dry, brittle, powder-like or pliable material, or for liquid or sticky material, does not govern a material that must be scraped to be tested. Each row's basis_guard pins the EU alternative to the category or categories that row's scope actually spans, so a flagged sibling value could not have anchored the cell even if it had been named. Where a row's scope genuinely spans more than one material category (toys-stuffing-materials spans Categories I and III; art-craft-materials and toys-crafts-other span all three), every applicable category row IS named in gov_ids, the strictest binds, and the full candidate set is disclosed in the compiled record. (2) THE THREE-INSTRUMENT MIGRATION FAMILY. Directive 2009/48/EC point 13, ASTM F963-17 4.3.5 (mandatory in the US under CPSIA s.106 / 16 CFR 1250) and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 state the same measured quantity in three phrasings, so all three are pooled and the strictest binds. For total arsenic and total mercury the ASTM and NOM-252 figures (25 and 60 mg/kg) are stricter than the Directive's Category III values (47 and 94 mg/kg) and therefore bind, which is also the position published on wiki/regulations/astm-f963-soluble-migration and wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. A prior revision of this config rejected the ASTM figures on the argument that ASTM states its limits in the legacy lineage whose conformity rule subtracts a per-element analytical correction, making them looser as enforced. THAT ARGUMENT IS WITHDRAWN AS UNSUBSTANTIATED: the source cited for it documents the correction for ISO 8124-3:2020 and attributes none to ASTM, and its ASTM statement is a limit-value lineage claim that ends at F963-07, before the editions relied on. HMTc does not publish a ceiling above a CPSIA-mandatory figure on an unsubstantiated reading of a conformity clause. For total chromium, where the Directive sets Cr(III) and Cr(VI) limits and no total-chromium limit, ASTM and NOM-252 agree at 60 mg/kg for general toy material, and NOM-252's stricter 25 mg/kg tier for preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint binds the two rows whose scope names those forms. (3) CANADIAN TOTAL-MERCURY AND TOTAL-LEAD COATING LIMITS. SOR/2011-17 and SOR/2016-193 cap TOTAL mercury at 10 mg/kg and total lead at 90 mg/kg in surface coating material. Those are content ceilings, not migration ceilings; each is a real and binding parallel obligation, each is disclosed on the relevant cell, and each is excluded from the migration cells by the basis_guard because a content limit and a migration limit are different measured quantities. REGISTER GAPS RECORDED, NOT WORKED AROUND. Three sovereign values that would bear on this category are absent from data/evidence/regulatory_limits.csv and are returned for ingestion through the verified regulatory-fetch pathway rather than invented here: (a) the EU Cr(III) migration rows of Directive 2009/48/EC point 13 (verified absent — the register carries no Cr-III species at all); (b) the EU ORGANIC-TIN migration rows of the same point 13 table, 0.9 / 0.2 / 12 mg/kg for Categories I / II / III, which are the health-relevant tin ceiling and are 15 000 times lower than the Category III total-tin figure this config publishes as a screen; and (c) the REACH Annex XVII entry 27 nickel-release limit of 0.5 ug/cm2/week, which is a per-area release rate in a third basis. A fourth item previously recorded as a gap is withdrawn: the 25 mg/kg modelling-clay and finger-paint chromium value IS in the register, as the Mexican statutory row mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25, and it now binds the two rows whose scope names those material forms. NOT CONVERTED INTO NUMBERS. The US LHAMA regime (15 U.S.C. 1277 / 16 CFR 1500.14(b)(8) via ASTM D-4236) is a chronic-hazard review and labelling duty with no numeric maximum level, and the mechanical requirements of EN 71-1 and ASTM F963-17 (small parts, magnet flux index, projectile kinetic energy, lid supports, balloon and marble warnings) are not metals limits. Neither is converted into a ceiling anywhere in this config.
Native basis family: Element MIGRATION from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C stomach-acid extraction, expressed in mg/kg of toy material. Three sovereign instruments state this same quantity in three phrasings — Directive 2009/48/EC point 13 element migration, split by the Directive's three material categories; ASTM F963-17 4.3.5 soluble (migrated) element; and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 bioavailable soluble element — and all three are pooled in the strictest-selection. This is never conflated with total element CONTENT of the material (CPSIA s.101 lead, RoHS homogeneous material, REACH Annex XVII cadmium and organotin, the consumer-textile restriction, Canadian SOR total lead and total mercury in surface coating material), which is a different measured quantity; content instruments appear only as companion obligations in substantiation and are excluded from every cell by a per-row basis_guard anchored at the start of the register's basis string..
Credible-regulator set: EU, UK, CODEX, international, US, AU-NZ, CA, HK, MX.
Credible-set note: MX (Secretaria de Salud / COFEPRIS) is admitted to the credible-regulator set for this category by governance ruling. NOM-252-SSA1-2011, mandatory since 2012-08-13, is Mexico's federal toy and school-article heavy-metals standard. Its Table 1 sets maximum bioavailable soluble-element limits for eight elements on the same acid-extraction quantity as EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 migration, in two scope tiers: any toy or school-article material except modelling clay and finger paint, and preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint. It is a federal instrument of general application enforced by COFEPRIS, not a sub-national or advisory measure, it carries its own Heavy Metal Index regulation page (wiki/regulations/mexico-nom-252-ssa1-2011-toy-school-article-metals), and the regulation pages for BOTH instruments this category otherwise relies on already name it as a member of the toy-migration family whose strictest value binds. Admitting MX is what lets the audit scan see its twelve statutory rows: before this change the scan gated on the default credible set, so NOM-252 was invisible to it and three of its rows were stricter than values this config published (arsenic 25 mg/kg and mercury 60 mg/kg against 47 and 94; modelling-clay and finger-paint total chromium 25 mg/kg against 60). The addition can only tighten: NOM-252 enters the strictest-selection on the same footing as every other named instrument and never sets a ceiling above one already published.
Provenance tags: regulatory-alignment | read-across | not-material | control | ratchet.
STAFF / HMTc content — not public wiki (CLAUDE.md Part 19 firewall). Generated from
data/evidence/regulatory_limits.csv via tools/standards/compile-category.mjs + cat21-config.json.
Version: cat21-v1.1 (2026-08-17)
### toys-painted — General toys with paint/surface coatings
- native basis: element MIGRATION from scraped-off toy material (EN 71-3 Category III, mg/kg of material) — a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling
- material: Cured surface coating: paint, varnish, lacquer, enamel, printing ink, screen-print, hot-stamp foil, applied decal, sticker and film.
- component-level sampling plan: Sample each distinct colour and each distinct coating system separately (a multi-colour print is multiple test portions). Scrape the coating to substrate over at least 100 mg per colour without entraining substrate. Sample the coating on any small part or removable accessory separately. Record coating thickness and whether the coating is accessible before and after the use-and-abuse conditioning of ASTM F963-17 8.5 / EN 71-1.

| analyte | value (µg/kg) | provenance | binding source | component logic |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Pb | 23000 | regulatory-alignment | eu_2009_48_cat3_pb_toy_migration (EU) | 23000 [EU]; 90000 [US]; 90000 [MX] |
| Cd | 17000 | regulatory-alignment | eu_2009_48_cat3_cd_toy_migration (EU) | 17000 [EU]; 75000 [US]; 75000 [MX] |
| tAs | 25000 | regulatory-alignment | astm_f963_tas_soluble (US) | 25000 [US]; 25000 [MX]; 47000 [EU] |
| iAs | n/m | not-material | bounded by tAs | No sovereign toy-safety instrument sets an inorganic-arsenic limit. Di |
| tHg | 60000 | regulatory-alignment | astm_f963_thg_soluble (US) | 60000 [US]; 60000 [MX]; 94000 [EU] |
| MeHg | n/m | not-material | bounded by tHg | Methylmercury is produced by microbial methylation in aquatic systems  |
| Ni | 930000 | regulatory-alignment | eu_2009_48_cat3_ni_toy_migration (EU) | 930000 [EU] |
| Sn | screen 180000000 | regulatory-alignment | eu_2009_48_cat3_sn_toy_migration (EU) | 180000000 [EU] |
| Al | 28130000 | regulatory-alignment | eu_2009_48_cat3_al_toy_migration (EU) | 28130000 [EU] |
| Cr | screen 60000 | regulatory-alignment | astm_f963_crtotal_soluble (US) | 60000 [US]; 60000 [MX] |
| Cr-VI | 53 | regulatory-alignment | eu_2009_48_cat3_crvi_toy_migration (EU) | 53 [EU] |

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. The Directive's Category III value binds at 23 mg/kg; the ASTM and NOM-252 rows are named so the strictest-selection covers the complete applicable family rather than a subset, and both are looser than the binding value. Total-lead CONTENT law runs in parallel on a different basis and is a companion obligation, not this cell's ceiling: CPSIA s.101 caps total lead at 100 mg/kg in any accessible children's-product substrate (cpsia_pb_childrens_product_substrate_100ppm) and at 90 mg/kg in the dried paint film (cpsia_pb_surface_coating_90ppm); Canada SOR/2016-193 and SOR/2011-17 set 90 mg/kg total lead in surface coating material and in applied stickers and films; Canada SOR/2018-83 sets 90 mg/kg total lead in accessible parts. HMTc requires conformity with both the migration ceiling published here and those content ceilings; the two are different measured quantities and are never combined into one number.
  - **Cd substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. The Directive's Category III value binds at 17 mg/kg; the ASTM and NOM-252 rows are named for completeness of the applicable family and are looser. REACH Annex XVII entry 23 as amended by Regulation (EU) 494/2011 restricts cadmium by total CONTENT (0.01 percent in listed polymers and in jewellery metal parts, 0.1 percent in paint and painted articles). Those are content ceilings on a different measured quantity and are far more permissive than the migration ceiling published here; they are recorded as companion obligations and are deliberately NOT used to set this cell, because publishing a 0.1-percent paint-content figure as the HMTc cadmium ceiling would replace a protective migration limit with a permissive content limit.
  - **tAs substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. Two of the three instruments set 25 mg/kg against the Directive's 47 mg/kg, so 25 mg/kg is the published ceiling and HMTc applies it to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. A previous revision of this config published the Directive's looser Category III figure instead, on the argument that ASTM states its soluble limits in the legacy EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 lineage, in which conformity is judged against an analytical-correction-adjusted result, so that ASTM's nominal figure is looser than the Directive's once both are put on a raw-result footing. THAT ARGUMENT IS WITHDRAWN AS UNSUBSTANTIATED. The only repository artifact cited for it, wiki/sources/iso2020-8124-3-toy-migration-elements.md, documents the Table 2 analytical correction for ISO 8124-3:2020 and says of ASTM only that F963 4.3 sits in the same lineage and used the legacy EN 71-3 limits unchanged through F963-07 — a limit-VALUE lineage statement that expressly ends before the F963-08 and later editions this cell relies on, and it attributes no analytical correction to ASTM at all. The register row's own notes record only that the values are unchanged across F963-08/-11/-16/-17/-23. HMTc does not publish a ceiling above a figure made mandatory by CPSIA s.106 / 16 CFR 1250 on an unsubstantiated reading of a conformity clause. If ASTM F963-17 4.3.5 / 8.3 is later ingested as a source page that quotes the conformity-decision rule and it does specify a correction, the question may be reopened on that record; until then the stricter figure binds, which is also the position already published on wiki/regulations/astm-f963-soluble-migration and wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. Arsenic is limited as the TOTAL element by every instrument in the family; inorganic arsenic has no migration counterpart and is handled on the iAs cell.
  - **tHg substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. Two of the three instruments set 60 mg/kg against the Directive's 94 mg/kg, so 60 mg/kg is the published ceiling and HMTc applies it to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. A previous revision of this config published the Directive's looser Category III figure instead, on the argument that ASTM states its soluble limits in the legacy EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 lineage, in which conformity is judged against an analytical-correction-adjusted result, so that ASTM's nominal figure is looser than the Directive's once both are put on a raw-result footing. THAT ARGUMENT IS WITHDRAWN AS UNSUBSTANTIATED. The only repository artifact cited for it, wiki/sources/iso2020-8124-3-toy-migration-elements.md, documents the Table 2 analytical correction for ISO 8124-3:2020 and says of ASTM only that F963 4.3 sits in the same lineage and used the legacy EN 71-3 limits unchanged through F963-07 — a limit-VALUE lineage statement that expressly ends before the F963-08 and later editions this cell relies on, and it attributes no analytical correction to ASTM at all. The register row's own notes record only that the values are unchanged across F963-08/-11/-16/-17/-23. HMTc does not publish a ceiling above a figure made mandatory by CPSIA s.106 / 16 CFR 1250 on an unsubstantiated reading of a conformity clause. If ASTM F963-17 4.3.5 / 8.3 is later ingested as a source page that quotes the conformity-decision rule and it does specify a correction, the question may be reopened on that record; until then the stricter figure binds, which is also the position already published on wiki/regulations/astm-f963-soluble-migration and wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. Separately, Canada SOR/2016-193 and SOR/2011-17 set 10 mg/kg TOTAL mercury in surface coating material and in applied stickers and films. That is a total-content ceiling on the coating, a different measured quantity from migration, and it is stricter than the migration ceiling published here. It is carried as a companion obligation rather than substituted into this cell, and HMTc requires conformity with BOTH: an article whose coating carries more than 10 mg/kg total mercury does not certify, whatever its migration result. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter mercury tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_thg_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row: that tier is scoped to preschool SCHOOL ARTICLES, modelling clay and finger paint, and NOM-252's general tier expressly covers any toy or school-article material EXCEPT modelling clay and finger paint, so a toy of this row's description sits in the general tier at 60 mg/kg. The stricter tier is adopted where the row's material genuinely is a school-craft, modelling-clay or finger-paint form: it binds on art-craft-materials, toys-crafts-other and toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft.
  - ?? **tHg stricter-unselected (same-basis audit):** 25000 µg/kg [MX] mx_nom252_2011_thg_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25 — basis "bioavailable soluble element from toy or school-article material; source unit mg/kg", scope "preschool school articles, modeling clay, and fi"
  - **Ni substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument that sets a numeric nickel ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate nickel at all, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no nickel limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The Directive's nickel figures are permissive relative to the other panel analytes and HMTc publishes them as written rather than substituting an occurrence-derived or invented value. REACH Annex XVII entry 27 additionally caps nickel RELEASE from articles in prolonged skin contact at 0.5 ug/cm2/week; that is a per-area release rate in a third basis, it is not represented in the register, and it is recorded as a companion obligation and a register gap rather than converted into a mg/kg figure here.
  - **Sn substantiation:** Directive 2009/48/EC Annex II Part III point 13 is the only instrument in the register that sets a numeric tin ceiling for toy material: ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate tin, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no tin limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The register holds only the Directive's three TOTAL-tin rows. The instrument itself sets TWO tin lines on the same migration basis: total tin (15 000 / 3 750 / 180 000 mg/kg for Categories I / II / III) and organic tin (0.9 / 0.2 / 12 mg/kg), the latter reproduced from the point 13 table on wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. A prior revision of this cell asserted that the Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric tin ceiling and disclosed only the REACH Annex XVII entry 20 organotin CONTENT restriction; that statement was wrong by omission, because it left the reader unaware that the same instrument caps organic tin at 12 mg/kg for this row's scraped-off (Category III) material while this cell publishes 180 000 mg/kg. The organic-tin migration rows are a recorded REGISTER GAP returned for ingestion through the verified regulatory-fetch pathway; once ingested, this row should gain a published organotin cell beside the total-tin screen, exactly as chromium carries a published Cr(VI) cell beside its total-chromium screen. Organotin is additionally restricted by REACH Annex XVII entry 20 on a total-CONTENT basis (0.1 percent as tin for dibutyltin, and for skin-contact and childcare articles dioctyltin); that is a different measured quantity and is a further companion obligation, not this cell's anchor.
  - **Al substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric aluminium ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate aluminium, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no aluminium limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The 2019/1922 amendment already tightened these values by roughly 2.5x; HMTc publishes the amended figures as written.
  - **Cr substantiation:** The EU Toy Safety Directive splits chromium into Cr(III) and Cr(VI) and sets no TOTAL-chromium ceiling, so the total-chromium value is taken over the two credible sovereign instruments that do set one on the acid-soluble migration basis. ASTM F963-17 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 agree at 60 mg/kg and HMTc applies that figure to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. NOM-252's stricter 25 mg/kg tier is scoped to preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint; none of those material forms is within this row's scope, so that tier is inapplicable here and it binds instead on art-craft-materials and toys-crafts-other, whose scopes name modelling clay, dough and finger paint expressly. TWO PRIOR STATEMENTS ON THIS CELL ARE WITHDRAWN. First, the assertion that ASTM applies a 30 percent analytical correction raising its enforced ceiling to about 86 mg/kg, so that HMTc's use of the nominal 60 mg/kg was over-compliance: that reading is unsubstantiated for the F963 editions relied on, for the reason recorded on the tAs cell, and 60 mg/kg is simply the instrument's figure applied as written. Second, the claim that the ISO 8124-3:2020 modelling-clay and finger-paint chromium limits of 25 mg/kg were a register gap: the same 25 mg/kg value for the same two material forms IS in the register as the Mexican statutory row mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25, and it now binds the rows it applies to. One genuine register gap remains against this cell and is recorded rather than worked around: the EU Cr(III) migration rows of Directive 2009/48/EC point 13 are absent from the register (verified — no Cr-III species exists anywhere in it) and are not invented here. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter chromium tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row for the reason stated above — the tier is scoped to preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint, none of which is within this row's scope — and it IS adopted on the three rows whose material forms fall inside it: art-craft-materials, toys-crafts-other and toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft.
  - ?? **Cr stricter-unselected (same-basis audit):** 25000 µg/kg [MX] mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25 — basis "bioavailable soluble element from toy or school-article material; source unit mg/kg", scope "preschool school articles, modeling clay, and fi"
  - **Cr-VI substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a Cr(VI)-specific ceiling for toy material (ASTM F963-17, ISO 8124-3:2020 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 all regulate TOTAL chromium only, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell; NOM-252's total-chromium rows are named on the Cr cell, never here). Cr(VI) never satisfies the total-chromium cell and the total-chromium screen never satisfies this cell. REACH Annex XVII entry 47 additionally caps Cr(VI) in leather articles at 3 mg/kg on a total-content basis; that content ceiling is a companion obligation for leather components and is not substituted into this migration cell.

### toys-substrate-materials — Toy substrate materials (accessible plastics/metals/woods/rubbers)
- native basis: element MIGRATION from scraped-off toy material (EN 71-3 Category III, mg/kg of material) — a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling
- material: Accessible polymer, elastomer, wood, metal, plated metal, paper/board, glass and ceramic substrate.
- component-level sampling plan: One test portion per distinct homogeneous substrate material, taken from accessible surfaces after use-and-abuse conditioning. Plated and painted parts are sampled twice: coating (routed to toys-painted) and substrate beneath. Metal fasteners, rivets, eyelets, springs and magnets are sampled as separate components.

Two of the three instruments set 60 mg/kg against the Directive's 94 mg/kg, so 60 mg/kg is the published ceiling and HMTc applies it to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. A previous revision of this config published the Directive's looser Category III figure instead, on the argument that ASTM states its soluble limits in the legacy EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 lineage, in which conformity is judged against an analytical-correction-adjusted result, so that ASTM's nominal figure is looser than the Directive's once both are put on a raw-result footing. The register row's own notes record only that the values are unchanged across F963-08/-11/-16/-17/-23. If ASTM F963-17 4.3.5 / 8.3 is later ingested as a source page that quotes the conformity-decision rule and it does specify a correction, the question may be reopened on that record; until then the stricter figure binds, which is also the position already published on wiki/regulations/astm-f963-soluble-migration and wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. Separately, Canada SOR/2016-193 and SOR/2011-17 set 10 mg/kg TOTAL mercury in surface coating material and in applied stickers and films. That is a total-content ceiling on the coating, a different measured quantity from migration, and it is stricter than the migration ceiling published here. It is carried as a companion obligation rather than substituted into this cell, and HMTc requires conformity with BOTH: an article whose coating carries more than 10 mg/kg total mercury does not certify, whatever its migration result. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter mercury tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_thg_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row: that tier is scoped to preschool SCHOOL ARTICLES, modelling clay and finger paint, and NOM-252's general tier expressly covers any toy or school-article material EXCEPT modelling clay and finger paint, so a toy of this row's description sits in the general tier at 60 mg/kg. The stricter tier is adopted where the row's material genuinely is a school-craft, modelling-clay or finger-paint form: it binds on art-craft-materials, toys-crafts-other and toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft.

  - **Ni substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument that sets a numeric nickel ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate nickel at all, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no nickel limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The Directive's nickel figures are permissive relative to the other panel analytes and HMTc publishes them as written rather than substituting an occurrence-derived or invented value. REACH Annex XVII entry 27 additionally caps nickel RELEASE from articles in prolonged skin contact at 0.5 ug/cm2/week; that is a per-area release rate in a third basis, it is not represented in the register, and it is recorded as a companion obligation and a register gap rather than converted into a mg/kg figure here.
  - **Sn substantiation:** Directive 2009/48/EC Annex II Part III point 13 is the only instrument in the register that sets a numeric tin ceiling for toy material: ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate tin, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no tin limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The register holds only the Directive's three TOTAL-tin rows. The instrument itself sets TWO tin lines on the same migration basis: total tin (15 000 / 3 750 / 180 000 mg/kg for Categories I / II / III) and organic tin (0.9 / 0.2 / 12 mg/kg), the latter reproduced from the point 13 table on wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. A prior revision of this cell asserted that the Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric tin ceiling and disclosed only the REACH Annex XVII entry 20 organotin CONTENT restriction; that statement was wrong by omission, because it left the reader unaware that the same instrument caps organic tin at 12 mg/kg for this row's scraped-off (Category III) material while this cell publishes 180 000 mg/kg. The organic-tin migration rows are a recorded REGISTER GAP returned for ingestion through the verified regulatory-fetch pathway; once ingested, this row should gain a published organotin cell beside the total-tin screen, exactly as chromium carries a published Cr(VI) cell beside its total-chromium screen. Organotin is additionally restricted by REACH Annex XVII entry 20 on a total-CONTENT basis (0.1 percent as tin for dibutyltin, and for skin-contact and childcare articles dioctyltin); that is a different measured quantity and is a further companion obligation, not this cell's anchor.
  - **Al substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric aluminium ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate aluminium, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no aluminium limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The 2019/1922 amendment already tightened these values by roughly 2.5x; HMTc publishes the amended figures as written.
  - **Cr substantiation:** The EU Toy Safety Directive splits chromium into Cr(III) and Cr(VI) and sets no TOTAL-chromium ceiling, so the total-chromium value is taken over the two credible sovereign instruments that do set one on the acid-soluble migration basis. ASTM F963-17 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 agree at 60 mg/kg and HMTc applies that figure to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. NOM-252's stricter 25 mg/kg tier is scoped to preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint; none of those material forms is within this row's scope, so that tier is inapplicable here and it binds instead on art-craft-materials and toys-crafts-other, whose scopes name modelling clay, dough and finger paint expressly. TWO PRIOR STATEMENTS ON THIS CELL ARE WITHDRAWN. First, the assertion that ASTM applies a 30 percent analytical correction raising its enforced ceiling to about 86 mg/kg, so that HMTc's use of the nominal 60 mg/kg was over-compliance: that reading is unsubstantiated for the F963 editions relied on, for the reason recorded on the tAs cell, and 60 mg/kg is simply the instrument's figure applied as written. Second, the claim that the ISO 8124-3:2020 modelling-clay and finger-paint chromium limits of 25 mg/kg were a register gap: the same 25 mg/kg value for the same two material forms IS in the register as the Mexican statutory row mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25, and it now binds the rows it applies to. One genuine register gap remains against this cell and is recorded rather than worked around: the EU Cr(III) migration rows of Directive 2009/48/EC point 13 are absent from the register (verified — no Cr-III species exists anywhere in it) and are not invented here. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter chromium tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row for the reason stated above — the tier is scoped to preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint, none of which is within this row's scope — and it IS adopted on the three rows whose material forms fall inside it: art-craft-materials, toys-crafts-other and toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft.
  - ?? **Cr stricter-unselected (same-basis audit):** 25000 µg/kg [MX] mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25 — basis "bioavailable soluble element from toy or school-article material; source unit mg/kg", scope "preschool school articles, modeling clay, and fi"
  - **Cr-VI substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a Cr(VI)-specific ceiling for toy material (ASTM F963-17, ISO 8124-3:2020 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 all regulate TOTAL chromium only, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell; NOM-252's total-chromium rows are named on the Cr cell, never here). Cr(VI) never satisfies the total-chromium cell and the total-chromium screen never satisfies this cell. REACH Annex XVII entry 47 additionally caps Cr(VI) in leather articles at 3 mg/kg on a total-content basis; that content ceiling is a companion obligation for leather components and is not substituted into this migration cell.

### toys-stuffed-bean-bag — Stuffed toys and bean bag-type toys
- native basis: element MIGRATION from scraped-off toy material (EN 71-3 Category III, mg/kg of material) — a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling
- material: Outer textile shell, pile, thread, printed fabric, plastic and metal attached features.
- component-level sampling plan: Separate test portions for: outer face fabric (per colour), pile, embroidery and applique thread, printed or screen-printed areas, plastic eyes/noses/safety joints, ribbons and woven labels, zips and metal fasteners. Seam-attached small parts are conditioned per EN 71-1 / ASTM F963-17 8.5 before accessibility is judged.

### toys-stuffing-materials — Stuffing materials
- native basis: element MIGRATION from accessible toy material (EN 71-3, mg/kg of material) — the stricter of the two material-category limit sets this row's scope spans (Category I) is published across the whole row; a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling
- material: Loose fill: fibre wadding, foam, granulate, pellets, micro-beads, ground shell, cereal/bean fill, weighting sand.
- component-level sampling plan: One test portion per distinct fill material and per distinct colour of dyed fill. Sample the fill as supplied inside the finished toy, not the pre-production lot. Where a toy contains a weighting pouch, the pouch fabric routes to toys-stuffed-bean-bag and the weighting medium is tested here.

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** Both named ids share the migration/soluble basis, so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. Total-lead CONTENT law runs in parallel on a different basis and is a companion obligation, not this cell's ceiling: CPSIA s.101 caps total lead at 100 mg/kg in any accessible children's-product substrate (cpsia_pb_childrens_product_substrate_100ppm) and at 90 mg/kg in the dried paint film (cpsia_pb_surface_coating_90ppm); Canada SOR/2016-193 and SOR/2011-17 set 90 mg/kg total lead in surface coating material and in applied stickers and films; Canada SOR/2018-83 sets 90 mg/kg total lead in accessible parts. HMTc requires conformity with both the migration ceiling published here and those content ceilings; the two are different measured quantities and are never combined into one number. REACH Annex XVII entry 72 as introduced by Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 caps lead, cadmium, arsenic and Cr(VI) at 1 mg/kg in the homogeneous material of consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles. Fill enclosed inside a toy is neither, so entry 72 is not read across as this row's ceiling; where a fibre fill is also supplied as a loose skin-contact textile, entry 72 binds it directly on a total-content basis and is a companion obligation. OVER-COMPLIANCE, DISCLOSED: this row's scope spans two of the Directive's material categories and the stricter of the two is published across the whole row, so for the row's Category III material forms (fibre wadding, foam slab, discrete pellets) the published value is stricter than the Directive's own value for those forms. ### toys-rattles — Rattles
- native basis: element MIGRATION from scraped-off toy material (EN 71-3 Category III, mg/kg of material) — a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling
- material: Moulded polymer, wood, metal or textile rattle body; internal pellets or beads; applied coating.
- component-level sampling plan: Separate test portions for shell/handle polymer or wood, applied coating (routed to toys-painted), internal pellets or beads, and any elastomer or textile grip. Internal pellets are tested even where they are not accessible in the as-supplied state, because rattle failure is the design hazard the mechanical standards address.
- cross-reference: Teething toys and teethers are HMTc Category 9 (Infant and Child Contact Products), not Category 21. A rattle marketed with a teething surface is certified against both rows.

Total-lead CONTENT law runs in parallel on a different basis and is a companion obligation, not this cell's ceiling: CPSIA s.101 caps total lead at 100 mg/kg in any accessible children's-product substrate (cpsia_pb_childrens_product_substrate_100ppm) and at 90 mg/kg in the dried paint film (cpsia_pb_surface_coating_90ppm); Canada SOR/2016-193 and SOR/2011-17 set 90 mg/kg total lead in surface coating material and in applied stickers and films; Canada SOR/2018-83 sets 90 mg/kg total lead in accessible parts. First, the assertion that ASTM applies a 30 percent analytical correction raising its enforced ceiling to about 86 mg/kg, so that HMTc's use of the nominal 60 mg/kg was over-compliance: that reading is unsubstantiated for the F963 editions relied on, for the reason recorded on the tAs cell, and 60 mg/kg is simply the instrument's figure applied as written. Second, the claim that the ISO 8124-3:2020 modelling-clay and finger-paint chromium limits of 25 mg/kg were a register gap: the same 25 mg/kg value for the same two material forms IS in the register as the Mexican statutory row mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25, and it now binds the rows it applies to. One genuine register gap remains against this cell and is recorded rather than worked around: the EU Cr(III) migration rows of Directive 2009/48/EC point 13 are absent from the register (verified — no Cr-III species exists anywhere in it) and are not invented here. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter chromium tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row for the reason stated above — the tier is scoped to preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint, none of which is within this row's scope — and it IS adopted on the three rows whose material forms fall inside it: art-craft-materials, toys-crafts-other and toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft.
  - ?? **Cr stricter-unselected (same-basis audit):** 25000 µg/kg [MX] mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25 — basis "bioavailable soluble element from toy or school-article material; source unit mg/kg", scope "preschool school articles, modeling clay, and fi"
  - **Cr-VI substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a Cr(VI)-specific ceiling for toy material (ASTM F963-17, ISO 8124-3:2020 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 all regulate TOTAL chromium only, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell; NOM-252's total-chromium rows are named on the Cr cell, never here). Cr(VI) never satisfies the total-chromium cell and the total-chromium screen never satisfies this cell. REACH Annex XVII entry 47 additionally caps Cr(VI) in leather articles at 3 mg/kg on a total-content basis; that content ceiling is a companion obligation for leather components and is not substituted into this migration cell.

### toys-squeeze — Squeeze toys
- native basis: element MIGRATION from scraped-off toy material (EN 71-3 Category III, mg/kg of material) — a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling
- material: Flexible PVC, vinyl, silicone, latex, TPE or foam body; printed decoration; internal squeaker.
- component-level sampling plan: Separate test portions for body elastomer (per colour), printed or pad-printed decoration (routed to toys-painted), squeaker or valve component, and any internal liquid or gel. Bath toys are sampled after the water-immersion conditioning required by EN 71-1.

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. The Directive's Category III value binds at 23 mg/kg; the ASTM and NOM-252 rows are named so the strictest-selection covers the complete applicable family rather than a subset, and both are looser than the binding value. Total-lead CONTENT law runs in parallel on a different basis and is a companion obligation, not this cell's ceiling: CPSIA s.101 caps total lead at 100 mg/kg in any accessible children's-product substrate (cpsia_pb_childrens_product_substrate_100ppm) and at 90 mg/kg in the dried paint film (cpsia_pb_surface_coating_90ppm); Canada SOR/2016-193 and SOR/2011-17 set 90 mg/kg total lead in surface coating material and in applied stickers and films; Canada SOR/2018-83 sets 90 mg/kg total lead in accessible parts. HMTc requires conformity with both the migration ceiling published here and those content ceilings; the two are different measured quantities and are never combined into one number.
  - **Cd substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. The Directive's Category III value binds at 17 mg/kg; the ASTM and NOM-252 rows are named for completeness of the applicable family and are looser. REACH Annex XVII entry 23 as amended by Regulation (EU) 494/2011 restricts cadmium by total CONTENT (0.01 percent in listed polymers and in jewellery metal parts, 0.1 percent in paint and painted articles). Those are content ceilings on a different measured quantity and are far more permissive than the migration ceiling published here; they are recorded as companion obligations and are deliberately NOT used to set this cell, because publishing a 0.1-percent paint-content figure as the HMTc cadmium ceiling would replace a protective migration limit with a permissive content limit.
  - **tAs substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. Two of the three instruments set 25 mg/kg against the Directive's 47 mg/kg, so 25 mg/kg is the published ceiling and HMTc applies it to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. A previous revision of this config published the Directive's looser Category III figure instead, on the argument that ASTM states its soluble limits in the legacy EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 lineage, in which conformity is judged against an analytical-correction-adjusted result, so that ASTM's nominal figure is looser than the Directive's once both are put on a raw-result footing. THAT ARGUMENT IS WITHDRAWN AS UNSUBSTANTIATED. The only repository artifact cited for it, wiki/sources/iso2020-8124-3-toy-migration-elements.md, documents the Table 2 analytical correction for ISO 8124-3:2020 and says of ASTM only that F963 4.3 sits in the same lineage and used the legacy EN 71-3 limits unchanged through F963-07 — a limit-VALUE lineage statement that expressly ends before the F963-08 and later editions this cell relies on, and it attributes no analytical correction to ASTM at all. The register row's own notes record only that the values are unchanged across F963-08/-11/-16/-17/-23. HMTc does not publish a ceiling above a figure made mandatory by CPSIA s.106 / 16 CFR 1250 on an unsubstantiated reading of a conformity clause. If ASTM F963-17 4.3.5 / 8.3 is later ingested as a source page that quotes the conformity-decision rule and it does specify a correction, the question may be reopened on that record; until then the stricter figure binds, which is also the position already published on wiki/regulations/astm-f963-soluble-migration and wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. Arsenic is limited as the TOTAL element by every instrument in the family; inorganic arsenic has no migration counterpart and is handled on the iAs cell.

Separately, Canada SOR/2016-193 and SOR/2011-17 set 10 mg/kg TOTAL mercury in surface coating material and in applied stickers and films. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter mercury tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_thg_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row: that tier is scoped to preschool SCHOOL ARTICLES, modelling clay and finger paint, and NOM-252's general tier expressly covers any toy or school-article material EXCEPT modelling clay and finger paint, so a toy of this row's description sits in the general tier at 60 mg/kg.   - **Ni substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument that sets a numeric nickel ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate nickel at all, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no nickel limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The Directive's nickel figures are permissive relative to the other panel analytes and HMTc publishes them as written rather than substituting an occurrence-derived or invented value. REACH Annex XVII entry 27 additionally caps nickel RELEASE from articles in prolonged skin contact at 0.5 ug/cm2/week; that is a per-area release rate in a third basis, it is not represented in the register, and it is recorded as a companion obligation and a register gap rather than converted into a mg/kg figure here.
  - **Sn substantiation:** Directive 2009/48/EC Annex II Part III point 13 is the only instrument in the register that sets a numeric tin ceiling for toy material: ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate tin, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no tin limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The register holds only the Directive's three TOTAL-tin rows. The instrument itself sets TWO tin lines on the same migration basis: total tin (15 000 / 3 750 / 180 000 mg/kg for Categories I / II / III) and organic tin (0.9 / 0.2 / 12 mg/kg), the latter reproduced from the point 13 table on wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. A prior revision of this cell asserted that the Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric tin ceiling and disclosed only the REACH Annex XVII entry 20 organotin CONTENT restriction; that statement was wrong by omission, because it left the reader unaware that the same instrument caps organic tin at 12 mg/kg for this row's scraped-off (Category III) material while this cell publishes 180 000 mg/kg. The organic-tin migration rows are a recorded REGISTER GAP returned for ingestion through the verified regulatory-fetch pathway; once ingested, this row should gain a published organotin cell beside the total-tin screen, exactly as chromium carries a published Cr(VI) cell beside its total-chromium screen. Organotin is additionally restricted by REACH Annex XVII entry 20 on a total-CONTENT basis (0.1 percent as tin for dibutyltin, and for skin-contact and childcare articles dioctyltin); that is a different measured quantity and is a further companion obligation, not this cell's anchor.
  - **Al substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric aluminium ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate aluminium, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no aluminium limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The 2019/1922 amendment already tightened these values by roughly 2.5x; HMTc publishes the amended figures as written.
  - **Cr substantiation:** The EU Toy Safety Directive splits chromium into Cr(III) and Cr(VI) and sets no TOTAL-chromium ceiling, so the total-chromium value is taken over the two credible sovereign instruments that do set one on the acid-soluble migration basis. ### toy-pacifiers — Toy pacifiers (toy-designated)
- native basis: element MIGRATION from scraped-off toy material (EN 71-3 Category III, mg/kg of material) — a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling
- material: Silicone or latex teat; rigid polymer shield and ring; clip, ribbon and chain.
- component-level sampling plan: Separate test portions for teat elastomer, shield and ring polymer (per colour), printed decoration, clip, and any ribbon or chain. Sample after the boiling and use-and-abuse conditioning specified by EN 71-1.
- cross-reference: Functional infant soothers and sucking/teething aids are HMTc Category 9 (pacifiers-and-sucking-teething-aids), reached via EN 1400 because Annex I item 16 excludes them from the Toy Safety Directive.

The instrument itself sets TWO tin lines on the same migration basis: total tin (15 000 / 3 750 / 180 000 mg/kg for Categories I / II / III) and organic tin (0.9 / 0.2 / 12 mg/kg), the latter reproduced from the point 13 table on wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. A prior revision of this cell asserted that the Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric tin ceiling and disclosed only the REACH Annex XVII entry 20 organotin CONTENT restriction; that statement was wrong by omission, because it left the reader unaware that the same instrument caps organic tin at 12 mg/kg for this row's scraped-off (Category III) material while this cell publishes 180 000 mg/kg. The organic-tin migration rows are a recorded REGISTER GAP returned for ingestion through the verified regulatory-fetch pathway; once ingested, this row should gain a published organotin cell beside the total-tin screen, exactly as chromium carries a published Cr(VI) cell beside its total-chromium screen. Organotin is additionally restricted by REACH Annex XVII entry 20 on a total-CONTENT basis (0.1 percent as tin for dibutyltin, and for skin-contact and childcare articles dioctyltin); that is a different measured quantity and is a further companion obligation, not this cell's anchor.
  - **Al substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric aluminium ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate aluminium, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no aluminium limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The 2019/1922 amendment already tightened these values by roughly 2.5x; HMTc publishes the amended figures as written.
  - **Cr substantiation:** The EU Toy Safety Directive splits chromium into Cr(III) and Cr(VI) and sets no TOTAL-chromium ceiling, so the total-chromium value is taken over the two credible sovereign instruments that do set one on the acid-soluble migration basis. ASTM F963-17 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 agree at 60 mg/kg and HMTc applies that figure to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. NOM-252's stricter 25 mg/kg tier is scoped to preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint; none of those material forms is within this row's scope, so that tier is inapplicable here and it binds instead on art-craft-materials and toys-crafts-other, whose scopes name modelling clay, dough and finger paint expressly. TWO PRIOR STATEMENTS ON THIS CELL ARE WITHDRAWN. First, the assertion that ASTM applies a 30 percent analytical correction raising its enforced ceiling to about 86 mg/kg, so that HMTc's use of the nominal 60 mg/kg was over-compliance: that reading is unsubstantiated for the F963 editions relied on, for the reason recorded on the tAs cell, and 60 mg/kg is simply the instrument's figure applied as written. Second, the claim that the ISO 8124-3:2020 modelling-clay and finger-paint chromium limits of 25 mg/kg were a register gap: the same 25 mg/kg value for the same two material forms IS in the register as the Mexican statutory row mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25, and it now binds the rows it applies to. One genuine register gap remains against this cell and is recorded rather than worked around: the EU Cr(III) migration rows of Directive 2009/48/EC point 13 are absent from the register (verified — no Cr-III species exists anywhere in it) and are not invented here. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter chromium tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row for the reason stated above — the tier is scoped to preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint, none of which is within this row's scope — and it IS adopted on the three rows whose material forms fall inside it: art-craft-materials, toys-crafts-other and toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft.
  - ?? **Cr stricter-unselected (same-basis audit):** 25000 µg/kg [MX] mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25 — basis "bioavailable soluble element from toy or school-article material; source unit mg/kg", scope "preschool school articles, modeling clay, and fi"
  - **Cr-VI substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a Cr(VI)-specific ceiling for toy material (ASTM F963-17, ISO 8124-3:2020 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 all regulate TOTAL chromium only, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell; NOM-252's total-chromium rows are named on the Cr cell, never here). Cr(VI) never satisfies the total-chromium cell and the total-chromium screen never satisfies this cell. REACH Annex XVII entry 47 additionally caps Cr(VI) in leather articles at 3 mg/kg on a total-content basis; that content ceiling is a companion obligation for leather components and is not substituted into this migration cell.

### toys-battery-electronic — Battery-operated/electronic toys
- native basis: element MIGRATION from scraped-off toy material (EN 71-3 Category III, mg/kg of material) — a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling
- material: Accessible polymer housing, keypad elastomer, display lens, cable sheath, plated contact, applied coating.
- component-level sampling plan: Separate test portions for housing polymer (per colour), keypad or button elastomer, display lens, cable sheath, accessible plated contacts and battery-compartment contacts, and applied coating. Battery-compartment security is verified per EN 71-1 / ASTM F963-17 before accessibility is judged. Inaccessible printed-circuit assemblies, solder, wiring and battery cells are assessed against the RoHS companion obligation, not against this row's migration ceilings.

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. The Directive's Category III value binds at 23 mg/kg; the ASTM and NOM-252 rows are named so the strictest-selection covers the complete applicable family rather than a subset, and both are looser than the binding value. Total-lead CONTENT law runs in parallel on a different basis and is a companion obligation, not this cell's ceiling: CPSIA s.101 caps total lead at 100 mg/kg in any accessible children's-product substrate (cpsia_pb_childrens_product_substrate_100ppm) and at 90 mg/kg in the dried paint film (cpsia_pb_surface_coating_90ppm); Canada SOR/2016-193 and SOR/2011-17 set 90 mg/kg total lead in surface coating material and in applied stickers and films; Canada SOR/2018-83 sets 90 mg/kg total lead in accessible parts. HMTc requires conformity with both the migration ceiling published here and those content ceilings; the two are different measured quantities and are never combined into one number.
  - **Cd substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. The Directive's Category III value binds at 17 mg/kg; the ASTM and NOM-252 rows are named for completeness of the applicable family and are looser. REACH Annex XVII entry 23 as amended by Regulation (EU) 494/2011 restricts cadmium by total CONTENT (0.01 percent in listed polymers and in jewellery metal parts, 0.1 percent in paint and painted articles). Those are content ceilings on a different measured quantity and are far more permissive than the migration ceiling published here; they are recorded as companion obligations and are deliberately NOT used to set this cell, because publishing a 0.1-percent paint-content figure as the HMTc cadmium ceiling would replace a protective migration limit with a permissive content limit.
  - **tAs substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. Two of the three instruments set 25 mg/kg against the Directive's 47 mg/kg, so 25 mg/kg is the published ceiling and HMTc applies it to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. A previous revision of this config published the Directive's looser Category III figure instead, on the argument that ASTM states its soluble limits in the legacy EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 lineage, in which conformity is judged against an analytical-correction-adjusted result, so that ASTM's nominal figure is looser than the Directive's once both are put on a raw-result footing. THAT ARGUMENT IS WITHDRAWN AS UNSUBSTANTIATED. The only repository artifact cited for it, wiki/sources/iso2020-8124-3-toy-migration-elements.md, documents the Table 2 analytical correction for ISO 8124-3:2020 and says of ASTM only that F963 4.3 sits in the same lineage and used the legacy EN 71-3 limits unchanged through F963-07 — a limit-VALUE lineage statement that expressly ends before the F963-08 and later editions this cell relies on, and it attributes no analytical correction to ASTM at all. The register row's own notes record only that the values are unchanged across F963-08/-11/-16/-17/-23. HMTc does not publish a ceiling above a figure made mandatory by CPSIA s.106 / 16 CFR 1250 on an unsubstantiated reading of a conformity clause. If ASTM F963-17 4.3.5 / 8.3 is later ingested as a source page that quotes the conformity-decision rule and it does specify a correction, the question may be reopened on that record; until then the stricter figure binds, which is also the position already published on wiki/regulations/astm-f963-soluble-migration and wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. Arsenic is limited as the TOTAL element by every instrument in the family; inorganic arsenic has no migration counterpart and is handled on the iAs cell.
  - **tHg substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. If ASTM F963-17 4.3.5 / 8.3 is later ingested as a source page that quotes the conformity-decision rule and it does specify a correction, the question may be reopened on that record; until then the stricter figure binds, which is also the position already published on wiki/regulations/astm-f963-soluble-migration and wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. Separately, Canada SOR/2016-193 and SOR/2011-17 set 10 mg/kg TOTAL mercury in surface coating material and in applied stickers and films. That is a total-content ceiling on the coating, a different measured quantity from migration, and it is stricter than the migration ceiling published here. It is carried as a companion obligation rather than substituted into this cell, and HMTc requires conformity with BOTH: an article whose coating carries more than 10 mg/kg total mercury does not certify, whatever its migration result. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter mercury tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_thg_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row: that tier is scoped to preschool SCHOOL ARTICLES, modelling clay and finger paint, and NOM-252's general tier expressly covers any toy or school-article material EXCEPT modelling clay and finger paint, so a toy of this row's description sits in the general tier at 60 mg/kg. The stricter tier is adopted where the row's material genuinely is a school-craft, modelling-clay or finger-paint form: it binds on art-craft-materials, toys-crafts-other and toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft.

### toys-sound-producing — Sound-producing toys
- native basis: element MIGRATION from scraped-off toy material (EN 71-3 Category III, mg/kg of material) — a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling

That is a total-content ceiling on the coating, a different measured quantity from migration, and it is stricter than the migration ceiling published here. It is carried as a companion obligation rather than substituted into this cell, and HMTc requires conformity with BOTH: an article whose coating carries more than 10 mg/kg total mercury does not certify, whatever its migration result. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter mercury tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_thg_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row: that tier is scoped to preschool SCHOOL ARTICLES, modelling clay and finger paint, and NOM-252's general tier expressly covers any toy or school-article material EXCEPT modelling clay and finger paint, so a toy of this row's description sits in the general tier at 60 mg/kg. The stricter tier is adopted where the row's material genuinely is a school-craft, modelling-clay or finger-paint form: it binds on art-craft-materials, toys-crafts-other and toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft.
  - ?? **tHg stricter-unselected (same-basis audit):** 25000 µg/kg [MX] mx_nom252_2011_thg_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25 — basis "bioavailable soluble element from toy or school-article material; source unit mg/kg", scope "preschool school articles, modeling clay, and fi"
  - **Ni substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument that sets a numeric nickel ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate nickel at all, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no nickel limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The Directive's nickel figures are permissive relative to the other panel analytes and HMTc publishes them as written rather than substituting an occurrence-derived or invented value. REACH Annex XVII entry 27 additionally caps nickel RELEASE from articles in prolonged skin contact at 0.5 ug/cm2/week; that is a per-area release rate in a third basis, it is not represented in the register, and it is recorded as a companion obligation and a register gap rather than converted into a mg/kg figure here.
  - **Sn substantiation:** Directive 2009/48/EC Annex II Part III point 13 is the only instrument in the register that sets a numeric tin ceiling for toy material: ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate tin, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no tin limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The register holds only the Directive's three TOTAL-tin rows. The instrument itself sets TWO tin lines on the same migration basis: total tin (15 000 / 3 750 / 180 000 mg/kg for Categories I / II / III) and organic tin (0.9 / 0.2 / 12 mg/kg), the latter reproduced from the point 13 table on wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. A prior revision of this cell asserted that the Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric tin ceiling and disclosed only the REACH Annex XVII entry 20 organotin CONTENT restriction; that statement was wrong by omission, because it left the reader unaware that the same instrument caps organic tin at 12 mg/kg for this row's scraped-off (Category III) material while this cell publishes 180 000 mg/kg. The organic-tin migration rows are a recorded REGISTER GAP returned for ingestion through the verified regulatory-fetch pathway; once ingested, this row should gain a published organotin cell beside the total-tin screen, exactly as chromium carries a published Cr(VI) cell beside its total-chromium screen. Organotin is additionally restricted by REACH Annex XVII entry 20 on a total-CONTENT basis (0.1 percent as tin for dibutyltin, and for skin-contact and childcare articles dioctyltin); that is a different measured quantity and is a further companion obligation, not this cell's anchor.
  - **Al substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric aluminium ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate aluminium, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no aluminium limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The 2019/1922 amendment already tightened these values by roughly 2.5x; HMTc publishes the amended figures as written.
  - **Cr substantiation:** The EU Toy Safety Directive splits chromium into Cr(III) and Cr(VI) and sets no TOTAL-chromium ceiling, so the total-chromium value is taken over the two credible sovereign instruments that do set one on the acid-soluble migration basis. ASTM F963-17 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 agree at 60 mg/kg and HMTc applies that figure to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. NOM-252's stricter 25 mg/kg tier is scoped to preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint; none of those material forms is within this row's scope, so that tier is inapplicable here and it binds instead on art-craft-materials and toys-crafts-other, whose scopes name modelling clay, dough and finger paint expressly. TWO PRIOR STATEMENTS ON THIS CELL ARE WITHDRAWN. First, the assertion that ASTM applies a 30 percent analytical correction raising its enforced ceiling to about 86 mg/kg, so that HMTc's use of the nominal 60 mg/kg was over-compliance: that reading is unsubstantiated for the F963 editions relied on, for the reason recorded on the tAs cell, and 60 mg/kg is simply the instrument's figure applied as written. Second, the claim that the ISO 8124-3:2020 modelling-clay and finger-paint chromium limits of 25 mg/kg were a register gap: the same 25 mg/kg value for the same two material forms IS in the register as the Mexican statutory row mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25, and it now binds the rows it applies to. One genuine register gap remains against this cell and is recorded rather than worked around: the EU Cr(III) migration rows of Directive 2009/48/EC point 13 are absent from the register (verified — no Cr-III species exists anywhere in it) and are not invented here. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter chromium tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row for the reason stated above — the tier is scoped to preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint, none of which is within this row's scope — and it IS adopted on the three rows whose material forms fall inside it: art-craft-materials, toys-crafts-other and toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft.

  - **Cr-VI substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a Cr(VI)-specific ceiling for toy material (ASTM F963-17, ISO 8124-3:2020 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 all regulate TOTAL chromium only, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell; NOM-252's total-chromium rows are named on the Cr cell, never here). Cr(VI) never satisfies the total-chromium cell and the total-chromium screen never satisfies this cell. REACH Annex XVII entry 47 additionally caps Cr(VI) in leather articles at 3 mg/kg on a total-content basis; that content ceiling is a companion obligation for leather components and is not substituted into this migration cell.

### toys-with-magnets — Toys with magnets
- native basis: element MIGRATION from scraped-off toy material (EN 71-3 Category III, mg/kg of material) — a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling
- material: Sintered NdFeB, ferrite or alnico magnet; Ni-Cu-Ni or epoxy plating; encapsulating polymer or wood housing.
- component-level sampling plan: Separate test portions for magnet body, plating stack, encapsulating housing polymer or wood, and applied coating. Magnets are tested both encapsulated as supplied and, where the encapsulation can be defeated by the use-and-abuse conditioning of EN 71-1 / ASTM F963-17, as a bare accessible component.

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. The Directive's Category III value binds at 23 mg/kg; the ASTM and NOM-252 rows are named so the strictest-selection covers the complete applicable family rather than a subset, and both are looser than the binding value. Total-lead CONTENT law runs in parallel on a different basis and is a companion obligation, not this cell's ceiling: CPSIA s.101 caps total lead at 100 mg/kg in any accessible children's-product substrate (cpsia_pb_childrens_product_substrate_100ppm) and at 90 mg/kg in the dried paint film (cpsia_pb_surface_coating_90ppm); Canada SOR/2016-193 and SOR/2011-17 set 90 mg/kg total lead in surface coating material and in applied stickers and films; Canada SOR/2018-83 sets 90 mg/kg total lead in accessible parts. HMTc requires conformity with both the migration ceiling published here and those content ceilings; the two are different measured quantities and are never combined into one number.
  - **Cd substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. The Directive's Category III value binds at 17 mg/kg; the ASTM and NOM-252 rows are named for completeness of the applicable family and are looser. REACH Annex XVII entry 23 as amended by Regulation (EU) 494/2011 restricts cadmium by total CONTENT (0.01 percent in listed polymers and in jewellery metal parts, 0.1 percent in paint and painted articles). Those are content ceilings on a different measured quantity and are far more permissive than the migration ceiling published here; they are recorded as companion obligations and are deliberately NOT used to set this cell, because publishing a 0.1-percent paint-content figure as the HMTc cadmium ceiling would replace a protective migration limit with a permissive content limit.
  - **tAs substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. Two of the three instruments set 25 mg/kg against the Directive's 47 mg/kg, so 25 mg/kg is the published ceiling and HMTc applies it to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. A previous revision of this config published the Directive's looser Category III figure instead, on the argument that ASTM states its soluble limits in the legacy EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 lineage, in which conformity is judged against an analytical-correction-adjusted result, so that ASTM's nominal figure is looser than the Directive's once both are put on a raw-result footing. The register row's own notes record only that the values are unchanged across F963-08/-11/-16/-17/-23. HMTc does not publish a ceiling above a figure made mandatory by CPSIA s.106 / 16 CFR 1250 on an unsubstantiated reading of a conformity clause. The register row's own notes record only that the values are unchanged across F963-08/-11/-16/-17/-23. Arsenic is limited as the TOTAL element by every instrument in the family; inorganic arsenic has no migration counterpart and is handled on the iAs cell.
