AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter mercury tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_thg_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row: that tier is scoped to preschool SCHOOL ARTICLES, modelling clay and finger paint, and NOM-252's general tier expressly covers any toy or school-article material EXCEPT modelling clay and finger paint, so a toy of this row's description sits in the general tier at 60 mg/kg. The stricter tier is adopted where the row's material genuinely is a school-craft, modelling-clay or finger-paint form: it binds on art-craft-materials, toys-crafts-other and toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft.
  - ?? **tHg stricter-unselected (same-basis audit):** 25000 µg/kg [MX] mx_nom252_2011_thg_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25 — basis "bioavailable soluble element from toy or school-article material; source unit mg/kg", scope "preschool school articles, modeling clay, and fi"
  - **Ni substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument that sets a numeric nickel ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate nickel at all, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no nickel limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The Directive's nickel figures are permissive relative to the other panel analytes and HMTc publishes them as written rather than substituting an occurrence-derived or invented value. REACH Annex XVII entry 27 additionally caps nickel RELEASE from articles in prolonged skin contact at 0.5 ug/cm2/week; that is a per-area release rate in a third basis, it is not represented in the register, and it is recorded as a companion obligation and a register gap rather than converted into a mg/kg figure here.
  - **Sn substantiation:** Directive 2009/48/EC Annex II Part III point 13 is the only instrument in the register that sets a numeric tin ceiling for toy material: ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate tin, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no tin limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The register holds only the Directive's three TOTAL-tin rows. The instrument itself sets TWO tin lines on the same migration basis: total tin (15 000 / 3 750 / 180 000 mg/kg for Categories I / II / III) and organic tin (0.9 / 0.2 / 12 mg/kg), the latter reproduced from the point 13 table on wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. A prior revision of this cell asserted that the Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric tin ceiling and disclosed only the REACH Annex XVII entry 20 organotin CONTENT restriction; that statement was wrong by omission, because it left the reader unaware that the same instrument caps organic tin at 12 mg/kg for this row's scraped-off (Category III) material while this cell publishes 180 000 mg/kg. The organic-tin migration rows are a recorded REGISTER GAP returned for ingestion through the verified regulatory-fetch pathway; once ingested, this row should gain a published organotin cell beside the total-tin screen, exactly as chromium carries a published Cr(VI) cell beside its total-chromium screen. Organotin is additionally restricted by REACH Annex XVII entry 20 on a total-CONTENT basis (0.1 percent as tin for dibutyltin, and for skin-contact and childcare articles dioctyltin); that is a different measured quantity and is a further companion obligation, not this cell's anchor.
  - **Al substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric aluminium ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate aluminium, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no aluminium limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The 2019/1922 amendment already tightened these values by roughly 2.5x; HMTc publishes the amended figures as written.
  - **Cr substantiation:** The EU Toy Safety Directive splits chromium into Cr(III) and Cr(VI) and sets no TOTAL-chromium ceiling, so the total-chromium value is taken over the two credible sovereign instruments that do set one on the acid-soluble migration basis. ASTM F963-17 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 agree at 60 mg/kg and HMTc applies that figure to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. NOM-252's stricter 25 mg/kg tier is scoped to preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint; none of those material forms is within this row's scope, so that tier is inapplicable here and it binds instead on art-craft-materials and toys-crafts-other, whose scopes name modelling clay, dough and finger paint expressly. TWO PRIOR STATEMENTS ON THIS CELL ARE WITHDRAWN. First, the assertion that ASTM applies a 30 percent analytical correction raising its enforced ceiling to about 86 mg/kg, so that HMTc's use of the nominal 60 mg/kg was over-compliance: that reading is unsubstantiated for the F963 editions relied on, for the reason recorded on the tAs cell, and 60 mg/kg is simply the instrument's figure applied as written. Second, the claim that the ISO 8124-3:2020 modelling-clay and finger-paint chromium limits of 25 mg/kg were a register gap: the same 25 mg/kg value for the same two material forms IS in the register as the Mexican statutory row mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25, and it now binds the rows it applies to. One genuine register gap remains against this cell and is recorded rather than worked around: the EU Cr(III) migration rows of Directive 2009/48/EC point 13 are absent from the register (verified — no Cr-III species exists anywhere in it) and are not invented here. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter chromium tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row for the reason stated above — the tier is scoped to preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint, none of which is within this row's scope — and it IS adopted on the three rows whose material forms fall inside it: art-craft-materials, toys-crafts-other and toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft.
  - ?? **Cr stricter-unselected (same-basis audit):** 25000 µg/kg [MX] mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25 — basis "bioavailable soluble element from toy or school-article material; source unit mg/kg", scope "preschool school articles, modeling clay, and fi"
  - **Cr-VI substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a Cr(VI)-specific ceiling for toy material (ASTM F963-17, ISO 8124-3:2020 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 all regulate TOTAL chromium only, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell; NOM-252's total-chromium rows are named on the Cr cell, never here). Cr(VI) never satisfies the total-chromium cell and the total-chromium screen never satisfies this cell. REACH Annex XVII entry 47 additionally caps Cr(VI) in leather articles at 3 mg/kg on a total-content basis; that content ceiling is a companion obligation for leather components and is not substituted into this migration cell.

### toys-balloons — Balloons
- native basis: element MIGRATION from scraped-off toy material (EN 71-3 Category III, mg/kg of material) — a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling
- material: Natural-rubber latex, polychloroprene or metallised polyester/nylon film; surface print; valve; tie.
- component-level sampling plan: Separate test portions for balloon wall (per colour), surface print or screen-print, self-sealing valve, and attached tie, ribbon or stick. Mouth-inflated balloons are treated as mouth-contact material for sampling priority.

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. The Directive's Category III value binds at 23 mg/kg; the ASTM and NOM-252 rows are named so the strictest-selection covers the complete applicable family rather than a subset, and both are looser than the binding value. Total-lead CONTENT law runs in parallel on a different basis and is a companion obligation, not this cell's ceiling: CPSIA s.101 caps total lead at 100 mg/kg in any accessible children's-product substrate (cpsia_pb_childrens_product_substrate_100ppm) and at 90 mg/kg in the dried paint film (cpsia_pb_surface_coating_90ppm); Canada SOR/2016-193 and SOR/2011-17 set 90 mg/kg total lead in surface coating material and in applied stickers and films; Canada SOR/2018-83 sets 90 mg/kg total lead in accessible parts. HMTc requires conformity with both the migration ceiling published here and those content ceilings; the two are different measured quantities and are never combined into one number.

Those are content ceilings on a different measured quantity and are far more permissive than the migration ceiling published here; they are recorded as companion obligations and are deliberately NOT used to set this cell, because publishing a 0.1-percent paint-content figure as the HMTc cadmium ceiling would replace a protective migration limit with a permissive content limit.

  - **Cd substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. The Directive's Category III value binds at 17 mg/kg; the ASTM and NOM-252 rows are named for completeness of the applicable family and are looser. REACH Annex XVII entry 23 as amended by Regulation (EU) 494/2011 restricts cadmium by total CONTENT (0.01 percent in listed polymers and in jewellery metal parts, 0.1 percent in paint and painted articles). Those are content ceilings on a different measured quantity and are far more permissive than the migration ceiling published here; they are recorded as companion obligations and are deliberately NOT used to set this cell, because publishing a 0.1-percent paint-content figure as the HMTc cadmium ceiling would replace a protective migration limit with a permissive content limit.
  - **tAs substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. Two of the three instruments set 25 mg/kg against the Directive's 47 mg/kg, so 25 mg/kg is the published ceiling and HMTc applies it to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. A previous revision of this config published the Directive's looser Category III figure instead, on the argument that ASTM states its soluble limits in the legacy EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 lineage, in which conformity is judged against an analytical-correction-adjusted result, so that ASTM's nominal figure is looser than the Directive's once both are put on a raw-result footing. THAT ARGUMENT IS WITHDRAWN AS UNSUBSTANTIATED. The only repository artifact cited for it, wiki/sources/iso2020-8124-3-toy-migration-elements.md, documents the Table 2 analytical correction for ISO 8124-3:2020 and says of ASTM only that F963 4.3 sits in the same lineage and used the legacy EN 71-3 limits unchanged through F963-07 — a limit-VALUE lineage statement that expressly ends before the F963-08 and later editions this cell relies on, and it attributes no analytical correction to ASTM at all. The register row's own notes record only that the values are unchanged across F963-08/-11/-16/-17/-23. HMTc does not publish a ceiling above a figure made mandatory by CPSIA s.106 / 16 CFR 1250 on an unsubstantiated reading of a conformity clause. If ASTM F963-17 4.3.5 / 8.3 is later ingested as a source page that quotes the conformity-decision rule and it does specify a correction, the question may be reopened on that record; until then the stricter figure binds, which is also the position already published on wiki/regulations/astm-f963-soluble-migration and wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. Arsenic is limited as the TOTAL element by every instrument in the family; inorganic arsenic has no migration counterpart and is handled on the iAs cell.
  - **tHg substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. If ASTM F963-17 4.3.5 / 8.3 is later ingested as a source page that quotes the conformity-decision rule and it does specify a correction, the question may be reopened on that record; until then the stricter figure binds, which is also the position already published on wiki/regulations/astm-f963-soluble-migration and wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter mercury tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_thg_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row: that tier is scoped to preschool SCHOOL ARTICLES, modelling clay and finger paint, and NOM-252's general tier expressly covers any toy or school-article material EXCEPT modelling clay and finger paint, so a toy of this row's description sits in the general tier at 60 mg/kg. The stricter tier is adopted where the row's material genuinely is a school-craft, modelling-clay or finger-paint form: it binds on art-craft-materials, toys-crafts-other and toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft.
  - ?? **tHg stricter-unselected (same-basis audit):** 25000 µg/kg [MX] mx_nom252_2011_thg_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25 — basis "bioavailable soluble element from toy or school-article material; source unit mg/kg", scope "preschool school articles, modeling clay, and fi"
  - **Ni substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument that sets a numeric nickel ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate nickel at all, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no nickel limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The Directive's nickel figures are permissive relative to the other panel analytes and HMTc publishes them as written rather than substituting an occurrence-derived or invented value. REACH Annex XVII entry 27 additionally caps nickel RELEASE from articles in prolonged skin contact at 0.5 ug/cm2/week; that is a per-area release rate in a third basis, it is not represented in the register, and it is recorded as a companion obligation and a register gap rather than converted into a mg/kg figure here.
  - **Sn substantiation:** Directive 2009/48/EC Annex II Part III point 13 is the only instrument in the register that sets a numeric tin ceiling for toy material: ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate tin, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no tin limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The register holds only the Directive's three TOTAL-tin rows. The instrument itself sets TWO tin lines on the same migration basis: total tin (15 000 / 3 750 / 180 000 mg/kg for Categories I / II / III) and organic tin (0.9 / 0.2 / 12 mg/kg), the latter reproduced from the point 13 table on wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. A prior revision of this cell asserted that the Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric tin ceiling and disclosed only the REACH Annex XVII entry 20 organotin CONTENT restriction; that statement was wrong by omission, because it left the reader unaware that the same instrument caps organic tin at 12 mg/kg for this row's scraped-off (Category III) material while this cell publishes 180 000 mg/kg. The organic-tin migration rows are a recorded REGISTER GAP returned for ingestion through the verified regulatory-fetch pathway; once ingested, this row should gain a published organotin cell beside the total-tin screen, exactly as chromium carries a published Cr(VI) cell beside its total-chromium screen. Organotin is additionally restricted by REACH Annex XVII entry 20 on a total-CONTENT basis (0.1 percent as tin for dibutyltin, and for skin-contact and childcare articles dioctyltin); that is a different measured quantity and is a further companion obligation, not this cell's anchor.
  - **Al substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric aluminium ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate aluminium, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no aluminium limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The 2019/1922 amendment already tightened these values by roughly 2.5x; HMTc publishes the amended figures as written.
  - **Cr substantiation:** The EU Toy Safety Directive splits chromium into Cr(III) and Cr(VI) and sets no TOTAL-chromium ceiling, so the total-chromium value is taken over the two credible sovereign instruments that do set one on the acid-soluble migration basis. ASTM F963-17 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 agree at 60 mg/kg and HMTc applies that figure to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. NOM-252's stricter 25 mg/kg tier is scoped to preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint; none of those material forms is within this row's scope, so that tier is inapplicable here and it binds instead on art-craft-materials and toys-crafts-other, whose scopes name modelling clay, dough and finger paint expressly. TWO PRIOR STATEMENTS ON THIS CELL ARE WITHDRAWN. First, the assertion that ASTM applies a 30 percent analytical correction raising its enforced ceiling to about 86 mg/kg, so that HMTc's use of the nominal 60 mg/kg was over-compliance: that reading is unsubstantiated for the F963 editions relied on, for the reason recorded on the tAs cell, and 60 mg/kg is simply the instrument's figure applied as written. Second, the claim that the ISO 8124-3:2020 modelling-clay and finger-paint chromium limits of 25 mg/kg were a register gap: the same 25 mg/kg value for the same two material forms IS in the register as the Mexican statutory row mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25, and it now binds the rows it applies to. One genuine register gap remains against this cell and is recorded rather than worked around: the EU Cr(III) migration rows of Directive 2009/48/EC point 13 are absent from the register (verified — no Cr-III species exists anywhere in it) and are not invented here. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter chromium tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row for the reason stated above — the tier is scoped to preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint, none of which is within this row's scope — and it IS adopted on the three rows whose material forms fall inside it: art-craft-materials, toys-crafts-other and toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft.
  - ?? **Cr stricter-unselected (same-basis audit):** 25000 µg/kg [MX] mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25 — basis "bioavailable soluble element from toy or school-article material; source unit mg/kg", scope "preschool school articles, modeling clay, and fi"
  - **Cr-VI substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a Cr(VI)-specific ceiling for toy material (ASTM F963-17, ISO 8124-3:2020 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 all regulate TOTAL chromium only, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell; NOM-252's total-chromium rows are named on the Cr cell, never here). Cr(VI) never satisfies the total-chromium cell and the total-chromium screen never satisfies this cell. REACH Annex XVII entry 47 additionally caps Cr(VI) in leather articles at 3 mg/kg on a total-content basis; that content ceiling is a companion obligation for leather components and is not substituted into this migration cell.

### toys-marbles — Marbles
- native basis: element MIGRATION from scraped-off toy material (EN 71-3 Category III, mg/kg of material) — a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling
- material: Soda-lime or borosilicate glass; internal colour cane or vane; ceramic body and glaze; polymer marble.
- component-level sampling plan: Separate test portions for glass body, internal colour cane or vane (the pigmented phase, which is where metals concentrate in glass marbles), ceramic body, ceramic glaze, and any applied surface coating or metallic lustre.

### toys-yo-yo-elastic-tether — Yo-yo elastic tether toys
- native basis: element MIGRATION from scraped-off toy material (EN 71-3 Category III, mg/kg of material) — a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling
- material: Elastomer or elasticated-cord tether; PVC, TPE or filled-gel body; finger loop; printed decoration.
- component-level sampling plan: Separate test portions for tether elastomer or elasticated cord, body polymer (per colour), finger loop, any internal fill or gel, and printed decoration. An accessible internal liquid or gel routes to toys-crafts-other, which publishes the Category II ceilings.

| analyte | value (µg/kg) | provenance | binding source | component logic |
|---|---|---|---|---|

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. The Directive's Category III value binds at 23 mg/kg; the ASTM and NOM-252 rows are named so the strictest-selection covers the complete applicable family rather than a subset, and both are looser than the binding value. Total-lead CONTENT law runs in parallel on a different basis and is a companion obligation, not this cell's ceiling: CPSIA s.101 caps total lead at 100 mg/kg in any accessible children's-product substrate (cpsia_pb_childrens_product_substrate_100ppm) and at 90 mg/kg in the dried paint film (cpsia_pb_surface_coating_90ppm); Canada SOR/2016-193 and SOR/2011-17 set 90 mg/kg total lead in surface coating material and in applied stickers and films; Canada SOR/2018-83 sets 90 mg/kg total lead in accessible parts. HMTc requires conformity with both the migration ceiling published here and those content ceilings; the two are different measured quantities and are never combined into one number.
  - **Cd substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. The Directive's Category III value binds at 17 mg/kg; the ASTM and NOM-252 rows are named for completeness of the applicable family and are looser. REACH Annex XVII entry 23 as amended by Regulation (EU) 494/2011 restricts cadmium by total CONTENT (0.01 percent in listed polymers and in jewellery metal parts, 0.1 percent in paint and painted articles). Those are content ceilings on a different measured quantity and are far more permissive than the migration ceiling published here; they are recorded as companion obligations and are deliberately NOT used to set this cell, because publishing a 0.1-percent paint-content figure as the HMTc cadmium ceiling would replace a protective migration limit with a permissive content limit.
  - **tAs substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. Two of the three instruments set 25 mg/kg against the Directive's 47 mg/kg, so 25 mg/kg is the published ceiling and HMTc applies it to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. A previous revision of this config published the Directive's looser Category III figure instead, on the argument that ASTM states its soluble limits in the legacy EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 lineage, in which conformity is judged against an analytical-correction-adjusted result, so that ASTM's nominal figure is looser than the Directive's once both are put on a raw-result footing. THAT ARGUMENT IS WITHDRAWN AS UNSUBSTANTIATED. The only repository artifact cited for it, wiki/sources/iso2020-8124-3-toy-migration-elements.md, documents the Table 2 analytical correction for ISO 8124-3:2020 and says of ASTM only that F963 4.3 sits in the same lineage and used the legacy EN 71-3 limits unchanged through F963-07 — a limit-VALUE lineage statement that expressly ends before the F963-08 and later editions this cell relies on, and it attributes no analytical correction to ASTM at all. The register row's own notes record only that the values are unchanged across F963-08/-11/-16/-17/-23. HMTc does not publish a ceiling above a figure made mandatory by CPSIA s.106 / 16 CFR 1250 on an unsubstantiated reading of a conformity clause. If ASTM F963-17 4.3.5 / 8.3 is later ingested as a source page that quotes the conformity-decision rule and it does specify a correction, the question may be reopened on that record; until then the stricter figure binds, which is also the position already published on wiki/regulations/astm-f963-soluble-migration and wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. Arsenic is limited as the TOTAL element by every instrument in the family; inorganic arsenic has no migration counterpart and is handled on the iAs cell.
  - **tHg substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. Two of the three instruments set 60 mg/kg against the Directive's 94 mg/kg, so 60 mg/kg is the published ceiling and HMTc applies it to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. A previous revision of this config published the Directive's looser Category III figure instead, on the argument that ASTM states its soluble limits in the legacy EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 lineage, in which conformity is judged against an analytical-correction-adjusted result, so that ASTM's nominal figure is looser than the Directive's once both are put on a raw-result footing. THAT ARGUMENT IS WITHDRAWN AS UNSUBSTANTIATED. The only repository artifact cited for it, wiki/sources/iso2020-8124-3-toy-migration-elements.md, documents the Table 2 analytical correction for ISO 8124-3:2020 and says of ASTM only that F963 4.3 sits in the same lineage and used the legacy EN 71-3 limits unchanged through F963-07 — a limit-VALUE lineage statement that expressly ends before the F963-08 and later editions this cell relies on, and it attributes no analytical correction to ASTM at all. The register row's own notes record only that the values are unchanged across F963-08/-11/-16/-17/-23. HMTc does not publish a ceiling above a figure made mandatory by CPSIA s.106 / 16 CFR 1250 on an unsubstantiated reading of a conformity clause. If ASTM F963-17 4.3.5 / 8.3 is later ingested as a source page that quotes the conformity-decision rule and it does specify a correction, the question may be reopened on that record; until then the stricter figure binds, which is also the position already published on wiki/regulations/astm-f963-soluble-migration and wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. Separately, Canada SOR/2016-193 and SOR/2011-17 set 10 mg/kg TOTAL mercury in surface coating material and in applied stickers and films. That is a total-content ceiling on the coating, a different measured quantity from migration, and it is stricter than the migration ceiling published here. It is carried as a companion obligation rather than substituted into this cell, and HMTc requires conformity with BOTH: an article whose coating carries more than 10 mg/kg total mercury does not certify, whatever its migration result. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter mercury tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_thg_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row: that tier is scoped to preschool SCHOOL ARTICLES, modelling clay and finger paint, and NOM-252's general tier expressly covers any toy or school-article material EXCEPT modelling clay and finger paint, so a toy of this row's description sits in the general tier at 60 mg/kg. The stricter tier is adopted where the row's material genuinely is a school-craft, modelling-clay or finger-paint form: it binds on art-craft-materials, toys-crafts-other and toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft.
  - ?? **tHg stricter-unselected (same-basis audit):** 25000 µg/kg [MX] mx_nom252_2011_thg_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25 — basis "bioavailable soluble element from toy or school-article material; source unit mg/kg", scope "preschool school articles, modeling clay, and fi"
  - **Ni substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument that sets a numeric nickel ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate nickel at all, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no nickel limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The Directive's nickel figures are permissive relative to the other panel analytes and HMTc publishes them as written rather than substituting an occurrence-derived or invented value. REACH Annex XVII entry 27 additionally caps nickel RELEASE from articles in prolonged skin contact at 0.5 ug/cm2/week; that is a per-area release rate in a third basis, it is not represented in the register, and it is recorded as a companion obligation and a register gap rather than converted into a mg/kg figure here.
  - **Sn substantiation:** Directive 2009/48/EC Annex II Part III point 13 is the only instrument in the register that sets a numeric tin ceiling for toy material: ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate tin, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no tin limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The register holds only the Directive's three TOTAL-tin rows. The instrument itself sets TWO tin lines on the same migration basis: total tin (15 000 / 3 750 / 180 000 mg/kg for Categories I / II / III) and organic tin (0.9 / 0.2 / 12 mg/kg), the latter reproduced from the point 13 table on wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. A prior revision of this cell asserted that the Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric tin ceiling and disclosed only the REACH Annex XVII entry 20 organotin CONTENT restriction; that statement was wrong by omission, because it left the reader unaware that the same instrument caps organic tin at 12 mg/kg for this row's scraped-off (Category III) material while this cell publishes 180 000 mg/kg. The organic-tin migration rows are a recorded REGISTER GAP returned for ingestion through the verified regulatory-fetch pathway; once ingested, this row should gain a published organotin cell beside the total-tin screen, exactly as chromium carries a published Cr(VI) cell beside its total-chromium screen. Organotin is additionally restricted by REACH Annex XVII entry 20 on a total-CONTENT basis (0.1 percent as tin for dibutyltin, and for skin-contact and childcare articles dioctyltin); that is a different measured quantity and is a further companion obligation, not this cell's anchor.
  - **Al substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric aluminium ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate aluminium, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no aluminium limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The 2019/1922 amendment already tightened these values by roughly 2.5x; HMTc publishes the amended figures as written.
  - **Cr substantiation:** The EU Toy Safety Directive splits chromium into Cr(III) and Cr(VI) and sets no TOTAL-chromium ceiling, so the total-chromium value is taken over the two credible sovereign instruments that do set one on the acid-soluble migration basis. ASTM F963-17 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 agree at 60 mg/kg and HMTc applies that figure to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. NOM-252's stricter 25 mg/kg tier is scoped to preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint; none of those material forms is within this row's scope, so that tier is inapplicable here and it binds instead on art-craft-materials and toys-crafts-other, whose scopes name modelling clay, dough and finger paint expressly. TWO PRIOR STATEMENTS ON THIS CELL ARE WITHDRAWN. First, the assertion that ASTM applies a 30 percent analytical correction raising its enforced ceiling to about 86 mg/kg, so that HMTc's use of the nominal 60 mg/kg was over-compliance: that reading is unsubstantiated for the F963 editions relied on, for the reason recorded on the tAs cell, and 60 mg/kg is simply the instrument's figure applied as written. Second, the claim that the ISO 8124-3:2020 modelling-clay and finger-paint chromium limits of 25 mg/kg were a register gap: the same 25 mg/kg value for the same two material forms IS in the register as the Mexican statutory row mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25, and it now binds the rows it applies to. One genuine register gap remains against this cell and is recorded rather than worked around: the EU Cr(III) migration rows of Directive 2009/48/EC point 13 are absent from the register (verified — no Cr-III species exists anywhere in it) and are not invented here. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter chromium tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row for the reason stated above — the tier is scoped to preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint, none of which is within this row's scope — and it IS adopted on the three rows whose material forms fall inside it: art-craft-materials, toys-crafts-other and toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft.
  - ?? **Cr stricter-unselected (same-basis audit):** 25000 µg/kg [MX] mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25 — basis "bioavailable soluble element from toy or school-article material; source unit mg/kg", scope "preschool school articles, modeling clay, and fi"
  - **Cr-VI substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a Cr(VI)-specific ceiling for toy material (ASTM F963-17, ISO 8124-3:2020 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 all regulate TOTAL chromium only, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell; NOM-252's total-chromium rows are named on the Cr cell, never here). Cr(VI) never satisfies the total-chromium cell and the total-chromium screen never satisfies this cell. REACH Annex XVII entry 47 additionally caps Cr(VI) in leather articles at 3 mg/kg on a total-content basis; that content ceiling is a companion obligation for leather components and is not substituted into this migration cell.

### toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft — Pompoms and similar small fibrous craft items
- native basis: element MIGRATION from scraped-off toy material (EN 71-3 Category III, mg/kg of material) — a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling
- material: Dyed acrylic, polyester, wool or cotton fibre; chenille stem wire core; felt; dyed feather; raffia.
- component-level sampling plan: Separate test portions per fibre type and per dye colour, plus the chenille stem wire core and any metallic or glitter-coated fibre tested separately. Deeply saturated reds, oranges and yellows are prioritised for sampling because those shades carry the highest historical metal-complex dye loading.

This row's material — pompoms, chenille stems and pipe cleaners, felt shapes, yarn, craft feathers, raffia and similar loose fibrous craft components — is supplied as preschool school-craft material, so NOM-252-SSA1-2011's preschool school-article tier is directly applicable and is adopted alongside its general tier. Where the two tiers differ the stricter binds. Deciding the other way would require arguing that a craft pompom sold into preschool use is not a school article; HMTc does not take that position, and the protective reading costs nothing. NOM-252's preschool school-article tier therefore binds this cell at 25 mg/kg, below the 60 mg/kg that ASTM F963-17 and the NOM-252 general tier set. TWO PRIOR STATEMENTS ON THIS CELL ARE WITHDRAWN. First, the assertion that ASTM applies a 30 percent analytical correction raising its enforced ceiling to about 86 mg/kg, so that HMTc's use of the nominal 60 mg/kg was over-compliance: that reading is unsubstantiated for the F963 editions relied on, for the reason recorded on the tAs cell, and 60 mg/kg is simply the instrument's figure applied as written. Second, the claim that the ISO 8124-3:2020 modelling-clay and finger-paint chromium limits of 25 mg/kg were a register gap: the same 25 mg/kg value for the same two material forms IS in the register as the Mexican statutory row mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25, and it now binds the rows it applies to. One genuine register gap remains against this cell and is recorded rather than worked around: the EU Cr(III) migration rows of Directive 2009/48/EC point 13 are absent from the register (verified — no Cr-III species exists anywhere in it) and are not invented here.
  - **Cr-VI substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a Cr(VI)-specific ceiling for toy material (ASTM F963-17, ISO 8124-3:2020 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 all regulate TOTAL chromium only, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell; NOM-252's total-chromium rows are named on the Cr cell, never here). Cr(VI) never satisfies the total-chromium cell and the total-chromium screen never satisfies this cell. REACH Annex XVII entry 47 additionally caps Cr(VI) in leather articles at 3 mg/kg on a total-content basis; that content ceiling is a companion obligation for leather components and is not substituted into this migration cell.

### toys-attached-cribs-playpens — Toys attached to cribs/playpens
- native basis: element MIGRATION from scraped-off toy material (EN 71-3 Category III, mg/kg of material) — a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling
- material: Polymer figures and panels; textile and plush elements; mirror backing; attachment strap, clamp and cord.
- component-level sampling plan: Separate test portions for figure and panel polymer (per colour), applied coating, textile and plush elements, mirror backing and frame, attachment strap webbing, clamp polymer, and any metal fastener or spring. The crib, cot or play yard itself is a durable nursery product outside Category 21.
- cross-reference: Teethers and teething toys attached to a crib or play-yard frame are HMTc Category 9 (Infant and Child Contact Products). The crib, cot or play yard itself is a durable nursery product and is not certified on this row.

  - **Pb substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. The Directive's Category III value binds at 23 mg/kg; the ASTM and NOM-252 rows are named so the strictest-selection covers the complete applicable family rather than a subset, and both are looser than the binding value. Total-lead CONTENT law runs in parallel on a different basis and is a companion obligation, not this cell's ceiling: CPSIA s.101 caps total lead at 100 mg/kg in any accessible children's-product substrate (cpsia_pb_childrens_product_substrate_100ppm) and at 90 mg/kg in the dried paint film (cpsia_pb_surface_coating_90ppm); Canada SOR/2016-193 and SOR/2011-17 set 90 mg/kg total lead in surface coating material and in applied stickers and films; Canada SOR/2018-83 sets 90 mg/kg total lead in accessible parts. HMTc requires conformity with both the migration ceiling published here and those content ceilings; the two are different measured quantities and are never combined into one number.
  - **Cd substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. The Directive's Category III value binds at 17 mg/kg; the ASTM and NOM-252 rows are named for completeness of the applicable family and are looser. REACH Annex XVII entry 23 as amended by Regulation (EU) 494/2011 restricts cadmium by total CONTENT (0.01 percent in listed polymers and in jewellery metal parts, 0.1 percent in paint and painted articles). Those are content ceilings on a different measured quantity and are far more permissive than the migration ceiling published here; they are recorded as companion obligations and are deliberately NOT used to set this cell, because publishing a 0.1-percent paint-content figure as the HMTc cadmium ceiling would replace a protective migration limit with a permissive content limit.
  - **tAs substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. Two of the three instruments set 25 mg/kg against the Directive's 47 mg/kg, so 25 mg/kg is the published ceiling and HMTc applies it to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. A previous revision of this config published the Directive's looser Category III figure instead, on the argument that ASTM states its soluble limits in the legacy EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 lineage, in which conformity is judged against an analytical-correction-adjusted result, so that ASTM's nominal figure is looser than the Directive's once both are put on a raw-result footing. THAT ARGUMENT IS WITHDRAWN AS UNSUBSTANTIATED. The only repository artifact cited for it, wiki/sources/iso2020-8124-3-toy-migration-elements.md, documents the Table 2 analytical correction for ISO 8124-3:2020 and says of ASTM only that F963 4.3 sits in the same lineage and used the legacy EN 71-3 limits unchanged through F963-07 — a limit-VALUE lineage statement that expressly ends before the F963-08 and later editions this cell relies on, and it attributes no analytical correction to ASTM at all. The register row's own notes record only that the values are unchanged across F963-08/-11/-16/-17/-23. HMTc does not publish a ceiling above a figure made mandatory by CPSIA s.106 / 16 CFR 1250 on an unsubstantiated reading of a conformity clause. If ASTM F963-17 4.3.5 / 8.3 is later ingested as a source page that quotes the conformity-decision rule and it does specify a correction, the question may be reopened on that record; until then the stricter figure binds, which is also the position already published on wiki/regulations/astm-f963-soluble-migration and wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. Arsenic is limited as the TOTAL element by every instrument in the family; inorganic arsenic has no migration counterpart and is handled on the iAs cell.

If ASTM F963-17 4.3.5 / 8.3 is later ingested as a source page that quotes the conformity-decision rule and it does specify a correction, the question may be reopened on that record; until then the stricter figure binds, which is also the position already published on wiki/regulations/astm-f963-soluble-migration and wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. Separately, Canada SOR/2016-193 and SOR/2011-17 set 10 mg/kg TOTAL mercury in surface coating material and in applied stickers and films. That is a total-content ceiling on the coating, a different measured quantity from migration, and it is stricter than the migration ceiling published here. It is carried as a companion obligation rather than substituted into this cell, and HMTc requires conformity with BOTH: an article whose coating carries more than 10 mg/kg total mercury does not certify, whatever its migration result. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter mercury tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_thg_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row: that tier is scoped to preschool SCHOOL ARTICLES, modelling clay and finger paint, and NOM-252's general tier expressly covers any toy or school-article material EXCEPT modelling clay and finger paint, so a toy of this row's description sits in the general tier at 60 mg/kg. The stricter tier is adopted where the row's material genuinely is a school-craft, modelling-clay or finger-paint form: it binds on art-craft-materials, toys-crafts-other and toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft.
  - ?? **tHg stricter-unselected (same-basis audit):** 25000 µg/kg [MX] mx_nom252_2011_thg_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25 — basis "bioavailable soluble element from toy or school-article material; source unit mg/kg", scope "preschool school articles, modeling clay, and fi"
  - **Ni substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument that sets a numeric nickel ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate nickel at all, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no nickel limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The Directive's nickel figures are permissive relative to the other panel analytes and HMTc publishes them as written rather than substituting an occurrence-derived or invented value. REACH Annex XVII entry 27 additionally caps nickel RELEASE from articles in prolonged skin contact at 0.5 ug/cm2/week; that is a per-area release rate in a third basis, it is not represented in the register, and it is recorded as a companion obligation and a register gap rather than converted into a mg/kg figure here.
  - **Sn substantiation:** Directive 2009/48/EC Annex II Part III point 13 is the only instrument in the register that sets a numeric tin ceiling for toy material: ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate tin, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no tin limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The register holds only the Directive's three TOTAL-tin rows. The instrument itself sets TWO tin lines on the same migration basis: total tin (15 000 / 3 750 / 180 000 mg/kg for Categories I / II / III) and organic tin (0.9 / 0.2 / 12 mg/kg), the latter reproduced from the point 13 table on wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. A prior revision of this cell asserted that the Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric tin ceiling and disclosed only the REACH Annex XVII entry 20 organotin CONTENT restriction; that statement was wrong by omission, because it left the reader unaware that the same instrument caps organic tin at 12 mg/kg for this row's scraped-off (Category III) material while this cell publishes 180 000 mg/kg. The organic-tin migration rows are a recorded REGISTER GAP returned for ingestion through the verified regulatory-fetch pathway; once ingested, this row should gain a published organotin cell beside the total-tin screen, exactly as chromium carries a published Cr(VI) cell beside its total-chromium screen. Organotin is additionally restricted by REACH Annex XVII entry 20 on a total-CONTENT basis (0.1 percent as tin for dibutyltin, and for skin-contact and childcare articles dioctyltin); that is a different measured quantity and is a further companion obligation, not this cell's anchor.
  - **Al substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric aluminium ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate aluminium, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no aluminium limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The 2019/1922 amendment already tightened these values by roughly 2.5x; HMTc publishes the amended figures as written.
  - **Cr substantiation:** The EU Toy Safety Directive splits chromium into Cr(III) and Cr(VI) and sets no TOTAL-chromium ceiling, so the total-chromium value is taken over the two credible sovereign instruments that do set one on the acid-soluble migration basis. One genuine register gap remains against this cell and is recorded rather than worked around: the EU Cr(III) migration rows of Directive 2009/48/EC point 13 are absent from the register (verified — no Cr-III species exists anywhere in it) and are not invented here. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter chromium tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. The instrument itself sets TWO tin lines on the same migration basis: total tin (15 000 / 3 750 / 180 000 mg/kg for Categories I / II / III) and organic tin (0.9 / 0.2 / 12 mg/kg), the latter reproduced from the point 13 table on wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. A prior revision of this cell asserted that the Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric tin ceiling and disclosed only the REACH Annex XVII entry 20 organotin CONTENT restriction; that statement was wrong by omission, because it left the reader unaware that the same instrument caps organic tin at 12 mg/kg for this row's scraped-off (Category III) material while this cell publishes 180 000 mg/kg. The organic-tin migration rows are a recorded REGISTER GAP returned for ingestion through the verified regulatory-fetch pathway; once ingested, this row should gain a published organotin cell beside the total-tin screen, exactly as chromium carries a published Cr(VI) cell beside its total-chromium screen. Organotin is additionally restricted by REACH Annex XVII entry 20 on a total-CONTENT basis (0.1 percent as tin for dibutyltin, and for skin-contact and childcare articles dioctyltin); that is a different measured quantity and is a further companion obligation, not this cell's anchor.
  - **Al substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric aluminium ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate aluminium, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no aluminium limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The 2019/1922 amendment already tightened these values by roughly 2.5x; HMTc publishes the amended figures as written.
  - **Cr substantiation:** The EU Toy Safety Directive splits chromium into Cr(III) and Cr(VI) and sets no TOTAL-chromium ceiling, so the total-chromium value is taken over the two credible sovereign instruments that do set one on the acid-soluble migration basis. ASTM F963-17 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 agree at 60 mg/kg and HMTc applies that figure to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. NOM-252's stricter 25 mg/kg tier is scoped to preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint; none of those material forms is within this row's scope, so that tier is inapplicable here and it binds instead on art-craft-materials and toys-crafts-other, whose scopes name modelling clay, dough and finger paint expressly. TWO PRIOR STATEMENTS ON THIS CELL ARE WITHDRAWN. First, the assertion that ASTM applies a 30 percent analytical correction raising its enforced ceiling to about 86 mg/kg, so that HMTc's use of the nominal 60 mg/kg was over-compliance: that reading is unsubstantiated for the F963 editions relied on, for the reason recorded on the tAs cell, and 60 mg/kg is simply the instrument's figure applied as written. Second, the claim that the ISO 8124-3:2020 modelling-clay and finger-paint chromium limits of 25 mg/kg were a register gap: the same 25 mg/kg value for the same two material forms IS in the register as the Mexican statutory row mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25, and it now binds the rows it applies to. One genuine register gap remains against this cell and is recorded rather than worked around: the EU Cr(III) migration rows of Directive 2009/48/EC point 13 are absent from the register (verified — no Cr-III species exists anywhere in it) and are not invented here. AUDIT ANSWER — NOM-252 PRESCHOOL TIER. The audit scan flags NOM-252-SSA1-2011's stricter chromium tier (25 mg/kg, mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25) as unselected. It is inapplicable to this row for the reason stated above — the tier is scoped to preschool school articles, modelling clay and finger paint, none of which is within this row's scope — and it IS adopted on the three rows whose material forms fall inside it: art-craft-materials, toys-crafts-other and toys-pompoms-fibrous-craft.

Cr(VI) never satisfies the total-chromium cell and the total-chromium screen never satisfies this cell. REACH Annex XVII entry 47 additionally caps Cr(VI) in leather articles at 3 mg/kg on a total-content basis; that content ceiling is a companion obligation for leather components and is not substituted into this migration cell.

### toy-chests — Toy chests
- native basis: element MIGRATION from scraped-off toy material (EN 71-3 Category III, mg/kg of material) — a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling
- material: Solid wood, plywood, MDF/particleboard, moulded plastic or sheet metal body; applied lacquer or paint; hinge, lid support, handle and caster.
- component-level sampling plan: Separate test portions for body material (wood, composite, plastic or metal), applied finish per colour (routed to toys-painted), applied decal or transfer, hinge and lid-support metal, handle, caster, and any textile or foam lining. Sample the interior surfaces as well as the exterior: a child sits inside a toy chest.

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. The Directive's Category III value binds at 23 mg/kg; the ASTM and NOM-252 rows are named so the strictest-selection covers the complete applicable family rather than a subset, and both are looser than the binding value. Total-lead CONTENT law runs in parallel on a different basis and is a companion obligation, not this cell's ceiling: CPSIA s.101 caps total lead at 100 mg/kg in any accessible children's-product substrate (cpsia_pb_childrens_product_substrate_100ppm) and at 90 mg/kg in the dried paint film (cpsia_pb_surface_coating_90ppm); Canada SOR/2016-193 and SOR/2011-17 set 90 mg/kg total lead in surface coating material and in applied stickers and films; Canada SOR/2018-83 sets 90 mg/kg total lead in accessible parts. HMTc requires conformity with both the migration ceiling published here and those content ceilings; the two are different measured quantities and are never combined into one number.
  - **Cd substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. The Directive's Category III value binds at 17 mg/kg; the ASTM and NOM-252 rows are named for completeness of the applicable family and are looser. REACH Annex XVII entry 23 as amended by Regulation (EU) 494/2011 restricts cadmium by total CONTENT (0.01 percent in listed polymers and in jewellery metal parts, 0.1 percent in paint and painted articles). Those are content ceilings on a different measured quantity and are far more permissive than the migration ceiling published here; they are recorded as companion obligations and are deliberately NOT used to set this cell, because publishing a 0.1-percent paint-content figure as the HMTc cadmium ceiling would replace a protective migration limit with a permissive content limit.
  - **tAs substantiation:** All named ids state one measured quantity — the acid-soluble element that migrates from accessible toy material under the EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 0.07 M HCl 37 C extraction, which NOM-252-SSA1-2011 calls bioavailable soluble element — so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. Two of the three instruments set 25 mg/kg against the Directive's 47 mg/kg, so 25 mg/kg is the published ceiling and HMTc applies it to the laboratory's analytical result as reported. A previous revision of this config published the Directive's looser Category III figure instead, on the argument that ASTM states its soluble limits in the legacy EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 lineage, in which conformity is judged against an analytical-correction-adjusted result, so that ASTM's nominal figure is looser than the Directive's once both are put on a raw-result footing. THAT ARGUMENT IS WITHDRAWN AS UNSUBSTANTIATED. The only repository artifact cited for it, wiki/sources/iso2020-8124-3-toy-migration-elements.md, documents the Table 2 analytical correction for ISO 8124-3:2020 and says of ASTM only that F963 4.3 sits in the same lineage and used the legacy EN 71-3 limits unchanged through F963-07 — a limit-VALUE lineage statement that expressly ends before the F963-08 and later editions this cell relies on, and it attributes no analytical correction to ASTM at all. The register row's own notes record only that the values are unchanged across F963-08/-11/-16/-17/-23. HMTc does not publish a ceiling above a figure made mandatory by CPSIA s.106 / 16 CFR 1250 on an unsubstantiated reading of a conformity clause. If ASTM F963-17 4.3.5 / 8.3 is later ingested as a source page that quotes the conformity-decision rule and it does specify a correction, the question may be reopened on that record; until then the stricter figure binds, which is also the position already published on wiki/regulations/astm-f963-soluble-migration and wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. Arsenic is limited as the TOTAL element by every instrument in the family; inorganic arsenic has no migration counterpart and is handled on the iAs cell.

### art-craft-materials — Art & craft materials (paints, markers, crayons, colored pencils, inks, adhesives/glues, modeling clay, putties/gels/slimes, glitter/pigments, jewelry/craft kits)
- native basis: element MIGRATION from accessible toy material (EN 71-3, mg/kg of material) — the strictest of the three material-category limit sets this row's scope spans (Category II) is published across the whole row; a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling
- material: Liquid and sticky: poster/tempera/finger paint, ink, marker fluid, glue, slime, putty, gel. Dry and pliable: crayon, chalk, pastel, pencil core, powder pigment, glitter, modelling clay and dough. Finished components: beads, findings, wire, cord.
- component-level sampling plan: Separate test portions per colour and per material form. Liquid and sticky materials are sampled as supplied without drying. Crayons, pastels and pencil cores are sampled as the pigmented core with the wooden casing and lacquer tested separately. Powder pigment and glitter are sampled undiluted. Modelling compounds are sampled per colour. Jewellery and craft-kit components are itemised: metal beads and findings, plated parts, glass and acrylic beads, cord and elastic, and any supplied tool.

  - **Pb substantiation:** Both named ids share the migration/soluble basis, so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. Total-lead CONTENT law runs in parallel on a different basis and is a companion obligation, not this cell's ceiling: CPSIA s.101 caps total lead at 100 mg/kg in any accessible children's-product substrate (cpsia_pb_childrens_product_substrate_100ppm) and at 90 mg/kg in the dried paint film (cpsia_pb_surface_coating_90ppm); Canada SOR/2016-193 and SOR/2011-17 set 90 mg/kg total lead in surface coating material and in applied stickers and films; Canada SOR/2018-83 sets 90 mg/kg total lead in accessible parts. HMTc requires conformity with both the migration ceiling published here and those content ceilings; the two are different measured quantities and are never combined into one number. Craft-kit jewellery components carry parallel total-CONTENT ceilings: Canada SOR/2018-82 caps total lead at 90 mg/kg in children's jewellery, Canada SOR/2018-83 caps total lead at 90 mg/kg in accessible parts of consumer products, and CPSIA s.101 caps total lead at 100 mg/kg in children's-product substrate. Those are content ceilings on a different measured quantity, all of them far more permissive than the 0.5 mg/kg migration ceiling published here, and they are companion obligations rather than this cell's anchor. OVER-COMPLIANCE, DISCLOSED: this row's scope spans all three of the Directive's material categories and the strictest of them is published across the whole row, so for the row's Category I material (crayons, chalk, pastel and pencil cores, powder pigment, glitter, modelling clay and dough) and Category III material (craft-kit beads, jewellery findings, wire, cord and moulded plastic or wooden parts) the published value is stricter than the Directive's own value for those forms. It is a deliberate protective choice, not those forms' legal ceiling; the compiled record's candidate set names every applicable category row with its value so the tag regulatory-alignment is read against the full disclosed set rather than as an assertion that Category II is this material's legal category. If ASTM F963-17 4.3.5 / 8.3 is later ingested as a source page that quotes the conformity-decision rule and it does specify a correction, the question may be reopened on that record; until then the stricter figure binds, which is also the position already published on wiki/regulations/astm-f963-soluble-migration and wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. On this row the ASTM and NOM-252 arsenic figures (25 mg/kg) are looser than the binding Category II value of 0.9 mg/kg and do not change the ceiling; they are named so the strictest-selection covers the complete applicable family. Arsenic is limited as the TOTAL element by every instrument in the family; inorganic arsenic has no migration counterpart and is handled on the iAs cell. OVER-COMPLIANCE, DISCLOSED: this row's scope spans all three of the Directive's material categories and the strictest of them is published across the whole row, so for the row's Category I material (crayons, chalk, pastel and pencil cores, powder pigment, glitter, modelling clay and dough) and Category III material (craft-kit beads, jewellery findings, wire, cord and moulded plastic or wooden parts) the published value is stricter than the Directive's own value for those forms. It is a deliberate protective choice, not those forms' legal ceiling; the compiled record's candidate set names every applicable category row with its value so the tag regulatory-alignment is read against the full disclosed set rather than as an assertion that Category II is this material's legal category. A previous revision of this config published the Directive's looser Category III figure instead, on the argument that ASTM states its soluble limits in the legacy EN 71-3 / ISO 8124-3 lineage, in which conformity is judged against an analytical-correction-adjusted result, so that ASTM's nominal figure is looser than the Directive's once both are put on a raw-result footing.   - **Ni substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument that sets a numeric nickel ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate nickel at all, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no nickel limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The Directive's nickel figures are permissive relative to the other panel analytes and HMTc publishes them as written rather than substituting an occurrence-derived or invented value. REACH Annex XVII entry 27 additionally caps nickel RELEASE from articles in prolonged skin contact at 0.5 ug/cm2/week; that is a per-area release rate in a third basis, it is not represented in the register, and it is recorded as a companion obligation and a register gap rather than converted into a mg/kg figure here. OVER-COMPLIANCE, DISCLOSED: this row's scope spans all three of the Directive's material categories and the strictest of them is published across the whole row, so for the row's Category I material (crayons, chalk, pastel and pencil cores, powder pigment, glitter, modelling clay and dough) and Category III material (craft-kit beads, jewellery findings, wire, cord and moulded plastic or wooden parts) the published value is stricter than the Directive's own value for those forms.   - **Sn substantiation:** Directive 2009/48/EC Annex II Part III point 13 is the only instrument in the register that sets a numeric tin ceiling for toy material: ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate tin, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no tin limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The register holds only the Directive's three TOTAL-tin rows. The instrument itself sets TWO tin lines on the same migration basis: total tin (15 000 / 3 750 / 180 000 mg/kg for Categories I / II / III) and organic tin (0.9 / 0.2 / 12 mg/kg), the latter reproduced from the point 13 table on wiki/regulations/eu-2009-48-toy-safety-migration-limits. A prior revision of this cell asserted that the Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric tin ceiling and disclosed only the REACH Annex XVII entry 20 organotin CONTENT restriction; that statement was wrong by omission, because it left the reader unaware that the same instrument caps organic tin at 0.2 mg/kg for this row's liquid or sticky (Category II) material while this cell publishes 3 750 mg/kg. The organic-tin migration rows are a recorded REGISTER GAP returned for ingestion through the verified regulatory-fetch pathway; once ingested, this row should gain a published organotin cell beside the total-tin screen, exactly as chromium carries a published Cr(VI) cell beside its total-chromium screen. Organotin is additionally restricted by REACH Annex XVII entry 20 on a total-CONTENT basis (0.1 percent as tin for dibutyltin, and for skin-contact and childcare articles dioctyltin); that is a different measured quantity and is a further companion obligation, not this cell's anchor. One genuine register gap remains against this cell: the EU Cr(III) migration rows of Directive 2009/48/EC point 13 are absent from the register (verified — no Cr-III species exists anywhere in it) and are not invented here.
  - **Cr-VI substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a Cr(VI)-specific ceiling for toy material (ASTM F963-17, ISO 8124-3:2020 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 all regulate TOTAL chromium only, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell; NOM-252's total-chromium rows are named on the Cr cell, never here). Cr(VI) never satisfies the total-chromium cell and the total-chromium screen never satisfies this cell. ### toys-crafts-other — Other toys/crafts (case-by-case)
- native basis: element MIGRATION from accessible toy material (EN 71-3, mg/kg of material) — the strictest of the three material-category limit sets this row's scope spans (Category II) is published across the whole row; a migration ceiling, not a total-content ceiling
- material: Determined per article at intake. Includes accessible liquids, gels, sticky and slime materials contained inside other toys.
- component-level sampling plan: Determined per article at intake and recorded in the certification file before testing. At minimum: every accessible homogeneous material, every applied coating, every liquid or gel fill, and every metal component, each as a separate test portion. Where the article maps onto an existing row, it is certified on that row instead and this row is not used.

| analyte | value (µg/kg) | provenance | binding source | component logic |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Pb | 500 | regulatory-alignment | eu_2009_48_cat2_pb_toy_migration (EU) | 500 [EU]; 2000 [EU]; 23000 [EU]; 90000 [US]; 90000 [MX]; 90000 [MX] |
| Cd | 300 | regulatory-alignment | eu_2009_48_cat2_cd_toy_migration (EU) | 300 [EU]; 1300 [EU]; 17000 [EU]; 50000 [MX]; 75000 [US]; 75000 [MX] |
| tAs | 900 | regulatory-alignment | eu_2009_48_cat2_tas_toy_migration (EU) | 900 [EU]; 3800 [EU]; 25000 [US]; 25000 [MX]; 25000 [MX]; 47000 [EU] |
| iAs | n/m | not-material | bounded by tAs | No sovereign toy-safety instrument sets an inorganic-arsenic limit. Di |
| tHg | 1900 | regulatory-alignment | eu_2009_48_cat2_thg_toy_migration (EU) | 1900 [EU]; 7500 [EU]; 25000 [MX]; 60000 [US]; 60000 [MX]; 94000 [EU] |
| MeHg | n/m | not-material | bounded by tHg | Methylmercury is produced by microbial methylation in aquatic systems  |
| Ni | 18800 | regulatory-alignment | eu_2009_48_cat2_ni_toy_migration (EU) | 18800 [EU]; 75000 [EU]; 930000 [EU] |
| Sn | screen 3750000 | regulatory-alignment | eu_2009_48_cat2_sn_toy_migration (EU) | 3750000 [EU]; 15000000 [EU]; 180000000 [EU] |
| Al | 560000 | regulatory-alignment | eu_2009_48_cat2_al_toy_migration (EU) | 560000 [EU]; 2250000 [EU]; 28130000 [EU] |
| Cr | screen 25000 | regulatory-alignment | mx_nom252_2011_cr_total_preschool_clay_fingerpaint_25 (MX) | 25000 [MX]; 60000 [US]; 60000 [MX] |
| Cr-VI | 5 | regulatory-alignment | eu_2009_48_cat2_crvi_toy_migration (EU) | 5 [EU]; 20 [EU]; 53 [EU] |

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** Both named ids share the migration/soluble basis, so the strictest-selection is basis-clean. Total-lead CONTENT law runs in parallel on a different basis and is a companion obligation, not this cell's ceiling: CPSIA s.101 caps total lead at 100 mg/kg in any accessible children's-product substrate and at 90 mg/kg in the dried paint film; Canada SOR/2016-193, SOR/2011-17 and SOR/2018-83 set 90 mg/kg total lead in surface coating material, applied stickers and films, and accessible parts. HMTc requires conformity with both the migration ceiling published here and those content ceilings.Where the article is a toy intended to come into contact with food (play tea and dinner sets, play food, toy cooking and baking sets), Directive 2009/48/EC additionally requires it to satisfy food-contact law. Regulation (EU) 10/2011 sets specific migration into food far below anything in this row — lead not detectable at 0.01 mg/kg food, cadmium at 0.002, mercury and arsenic at 0.01, total chromium at 0.01, nickel 0.02, aluminium 1 — on a migration-into-food basis that is a third measured quantity. It is a companion obligation for food-contact toys and is never resolved into this cell. OVER-COMPLIANCE, DISCLOSED: this row's scope spans all three of the Directive's material categories and the strictest of them is published across the whole row, so for the row's Category I material (any dry, brittle, powder-like or pliable material presented at intake) and Category III material (any scraped-off-type material presented at intake) the published value is stricter than the Directive's own value for those forms. It is a deliberate protective choice, not those forms' legal ceiling; the compiled record's candidate set names every applicable category row with its value so the tag regulatory-alignment is read against the full disclosed set rather than as an assertion that Category II is this material's legal category. STRUCTURAL NOTE: the durable fix is to split this row into per-material-category sub-rows, each with its own basis and its own Directive category. That requires new Category 21 subcategory slugs in data/taxonomy/hmtc-v2.json, which is a Step 0 taxonomy lock and outside a remediation pass; it is recorded as an outstanding item rather than performed here.
  - **Cd substantiation:** Both named ids share the migration/soluble basis. REACH Annex XVII entry 23 as amended by Regulation (EU) 494/2011 restricts cadmium by total CONTENT (0.01 percent in listed polymers and in jewellery metal parts, 0.1 percent in paint and painted articles). Those are content ceilings on a different measured quantity and are far more permissive than the migration ceiling published here; they are recorded as companion obligations and are deliberately NOT used to set this cell, because publishing a 0.1-percent paint-content figure as the HMTc cadmium ceiling would replace a protective migration limit with a permissive content limit.
