A prior revision of this cell asserted that the Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric tin ceiling and disclosed only the REACH Annex XVII entry 20 organotin CONTENT restriction; that statement was wrong by omission, because it left the reader unaware that the same instrument caps organic tin at 0.2 mg/kg for this row's liquid or sticky (Category II) material while this cell publishes 3 750 mg/kg. The organic-tin migration rows are a recorded REGISTER GAP returned for ingestion through the verified regulatory-fetch pathway; once ingested, this row should gain a published organotin cell beside the total-tin screen, exactly as chromium carries a published Cr(VI) cell beside its total-chromium screen. Organotin is additionally restricted by REACH Annex XVII entry 20 on a total-CONTENT basis (0.1 percent as tin for dibutyltin, and for skin-contact and childcare articles dioctyltin); that is a different measured quantity and is a further companion obligation, not this cell's anchor. OVER-COMPLIANCE, DISCLOSED: this row's scope spans all three of the Directive's material categories and the strictest of them is published across the whole row, so for the row's Category I material (any dry, brittle, powder-like or pliable material presented at intake) and Category III material (any scraped-off-type material presented at intake) the published value is stricter than the Directive's own value for those forms. It is a deliberate protective choice, not those forms' legal ceiling; the compiled record's candidate set names every applicable category row with its value so the tag regulatory-alignment is read against the full disclosed set rather than as an assertion that Category II is this material's legal category. STRUCTURAL NOTE: the durable fix is to split this row into per-material-category sub-rows, each with its own basis and its own Directive category. That requires new Category 21 subcategory slugs in data/taxonomy/hmtc-v2.json, which is a Step 0 taxonomy lock and outside a remediation pass; it is recorded as an outstanding item rather than performed here.
  - **Al substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a numeric aluminium ceiling for toy material; ASTM F963-17 and ISO 8124-3:2020 do not regulate aluminium, and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 Table 1 regulates antimony, arsenic, barium, cadmium, total chromium, lead, mercury and selenium and sets no aluminium limit, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell. The 2019/1922 amendment already tightened these values by roughly 2.5x; HMTc publishes the amended figures as written. OVER-COMPLIANCE, DISCLOSED: this row's scope spans all three of the Directive's material categories and the strictest of them is published across the whole row, so for the row's Category I material (any dry, brittle, powder-like or pliable material presented at intake) and Category III material (any scraped-off-type material presented at intake) the published value is stricter than the Directive's own value for those forms. It is a deliberate protective choice, not those forms' legal ceiling; the compiled record's candidate set names every applicable category row with its value so the tag regulatory-alignment is read against the full disclosed set rather than as an assertion that Category II is this material's legal category. STRUCTURAL NOTE: the durable fix is to split this row into per-material-category sub-rows, each with its own basis and its own Directive category. That requires new Category 21 subcategory slugs in data/taxonomy/hmtc-v2.json, which is a Step 0 taxonomy lock and outside a remediation pass; it is recorded as an outstanding item rather than performed here.
  - **Cr substantiation:** The EU Toy Safety Directive splits chromium into Cr(III) and Cr(VI) and sets no TOTAL-chromium ceiling, so the total-chromium value is taken over the two credible sovereign instruments that do set one on the acid-soluble migration basis. This row's scope names modelling clay, dough and finger paint expressly (and, for school articles, crayons, chalk, pastels and pencil cores), so NOM-252-SSA1-2011's preschool, modelling-clay and finger-paint tier is directly applicable and binds at 25 mg/kg, below the 60 mg/kg set by both its general tier and ASTM F963-17. A PRIOR STATEMENT ON THIS CELL IS WITHDRAWN AS AFFIRMATIVELY WRONG: earlier revisions published 60 mg/kg here and recorded the 25 mg/kg modelling-clay and finger-paint chromium value as an un-ingested register gap traceable only to ISO 8124-3:2020 Table 1. The same 25 mg/kg value for the same two material forms was already in the register as a Mexican statutory row, and it is now named and binds. The ASTM analytical-correction reading previously offered on this cell is also withdrawn as unsubstantiated for the F963 editions relied on, for the reason recorded on the tAs cell. OVER-COMPLIANCE, DISCLOSED: this row's scope spans all three of the Directive's material categories and the strictest of them is published across the whole row, so for the row's Category I material (any dry, brittle, powder-like or pliable material presented at intake) and Category III material (any scraped-off-type material presented at intake) the published value is stricter than the Directive's own value for those forms. It is a deliberate protective choice, not those forms' legal ceiling; the compiled record's candidate set names every applicable category row with its value so the tag regulatory-alignment is read against the full disclosed set rather than as an assertion that Category II is this material's legal category. STRUCTURAL NOTE: the durable fix is to split this row into per-material-category sub-rows, each with its own basis and its own Directive category. That requires new Category 21 subcategory slugs in data/taxonomy/hmtc-v2.json, which is a Step 0 taxonomy lock and outside a remediation pass; it is recorded as an outstanding item rather than performed here. For the row's non-clay, non-finger-paint, non-school-article forms (adhesives, slime, glitter, craft-kit components) the 25 mg/kg figure is stricter than the 60 mg/kg those forms carry under the general NOM-252 tier and ASTM, which is disclosed here as over-compliance in the protective direction. One genuine register gap remains against this cell: the EU Cr(III) migration rows of Directive 2009/48/EC point 13 are absent from the register (verified — no Cr-III species exists anywhere in it) and are not invented here.
  - **Cr-VI substantiation:** The Toy Safety Directive is the only credible sovereign instrument setting a Cr(VI)-specific ceiling for toy material (ASTM F963-17, ISO 8124-3:2020 and NOM-252-SSA1-2011 all regulate TOTAL chromium only, so admitting MX to the credible-regulator set does not add a candidate to this cell; NOM-252's total-chromium rows are named on the Cr cell, never here). Cr(VI) never satisfies the total-chromium cell and the total-chromium screen never satisfies this cell. REACH Annex XVII entry 47 additionally caps Cr(VI) in leather articles at 3 mg/kg on a total-content basis; that content ceiling is a companion obligation for leather components and is not substituted into this migration cell. OVER-COMPLIANCE, DISCLOSED: this row's scope spans all three of the Directive's material categories and the strictest of them is published across the whole row, so for the row's Category I material (any dry, brittle, powder-like or pliable material presented at intake) and Category III material (any scraped-off-type material presented at intake) the published value is stricter than the Directive's own value for those forms. It is a deliberate protective choice, not those forms' legal ceiling; the compiled record's candidate set names every applicable category row with its value so the tag regulatory-alignment is read against the full disclosed set rather than as an assertion that Category II is this material's legal category.
